  questions with SectionId == null (new `GET /api/questions?examId=
  &unassignedOnly=true` filter), Type/Difficulty filters + keyword
  search, checkbox multi-select with a running Selected Questions/Total
  Marks summary. "Add Section" (or "Save" on Edit) does one combined
  action: creates/updates the Section from Steps 1-2's state, then a new
  bulk `PUT /api/questions/bulk-assign-section` sets SectionId on every
  selected question in one request (avoids N sequential calls).
- Section Details / View page (wireframe screen 07): section info +
  assigned-questions table + "View All Questions" link + Edit Section
  button.
- Delete Section (confirm-modal pattern, matching DeleteQuestionButton/
  DeleteExamButton): clears SectionId back to null on its questions
  rather than deleting them, per Decision 5 above.
Day complete when: verified end-to-end for real - assigned questions to a
throwaway section, confirmed their SectionId in QuestionDb, deleted the
section, confirmed the same questions now show SectionId=null and
reappear in Manual Selection unassigned.

Backend (Exam Service): `Section` entity + `NavigationType` enum

(`/api/exams/{examId}/sections/**`, Admin-only). `IQuestionServiceClient`
(new Exam.Infrastructure HTTP client, mirrors `IExamLookupClient`'s
bearer-forwarding pattern) calls Question Service when a Section is
deleted, so its questions get unassigned rather than orphaned or
deleted - best-effort/logged-not-fatal, same principle as
`CreateAssignmentHandler`'s event publish.

Backend (Question Service): `ExamQuestion` gained nullable `SectionId`.
`GET /api/questions` gained `sectionId`/`unassignedOnly` query filters.
New `PUT /api/questions/bulk-assign-section` (Admin-only, sets/clears
SectionId on a batch of question ids in one call) and
`PUT /internal/questions/sections/{sectionId}/unassign` (added to the
existing internal-only controller, outside `/api` so the Gateway can
never proxy it - same reasoning as the Phase 8 answer-key endpoint;
this is what `IQuestionServiceClient` calls).

Frontend: `ManageSections` (list), `SectionForm` (one shared 3-step
wizard component for both Add and Edit - Information/Rules/Question
Assignment, single combined submit at the end rather than per-step API
calls), `SectionDetails` (view), `ReorderSections` (up/down-arrow
reordering rather than pulling in a new drag-and-drop dependency - a
deliberate scope simplification, no DnD library existed in package.json
and this app has a general no-extra-dependency preference). `CreateExam`
gained the Category dropdown (`EXAM_CATEGORIES`, a fixed short list, no
separate manageable entity) and the "This exam contains sections"
toggle. `ManageExams` gained the Category column + filter.
`ExamDetails` gained a conditional "Manage Sections" button
(`exam.containsSections`) and a Category field - it already carries the
title "Exam Review & Publish" from an earlier day, so it already serves
the wireframe's step-4 role without needing a new page. `EditExam`
gained a second "Exam Configuration" settings section (5 new
`SettingCard` toggles) using the exact same pattern its existing
Exam Settings section already used - reused rather than building a
redundant multi-step wizard shell around Create Exam, since Manage
Sections/Exam Configuration/Review&Publish already exist as their own
real screens (Manage Sections, Edit Exam, Exam Details respectively).
`TakeExam.tsx` (Submission Service's student-facing page) is the one
piece of Exam Configuration that's actually enforced, not just stored:
a `showSubmitConfirm` Modal now gates both Submit Exam buttons behind
`exam.confirmBeforeSubmit`, matching the existing Delete-confirmation
Modal pattern already used elsewhere (`DeleteSectionButton` etc.)
rather than a native `window.confirm`. `ShowSectionSummaryToStudents`/
`AllowCalculator`/`AllowNotes`/`AutoSubmitOnTimeEnd` are stored
preferences with no consumer yet - flagged in a code comment on
`ExamPaper`, not silently built or dropped, per the milestone's own
scope note above.

23 new backend tests (15 Exam Service: CreateSection
validator/handler, UpdateSection handler, DeleteSection handler incl. a
Question-Service-failure-does-not-fail-the-delete case, Reorder
handler; 2 Question Service: BulkAssignSection handler) - full backend
suite now 223/223 (up from 206), zero regressions. Frontend
`tsc`/`oxlint`/`npm run build`/26 Vitest tests all green (25 prior + 1
new Category-required validation case).

Verified live end-to-end twice: (1) via curl through the Gateway with a
real Admin JWT - created a sectioned exam (Category+ContainsSections

(SectionId null), bulk-assigned it (confirmed via both

same discipline as every prior day.

Operational note from this session: rebuilding the whole Docker stack
(`docker compose up -d --build` with no service argument) while it's
already running pushes this machine's ~8GB RAM into swap and can crash
buildkit mid-build - confirmed again here. Every rebuild this session
instead targeted only the changed service(s)
(`docker compose build exam-api question-api frontend`, then
`docker compose up -d` with no `--build` for the rest), which stayed
fast and never touched SQL Server/RabbitMQ/the other 8 already-running
containers.

(Azure Service Bus, Azure provisioning, CI/CD, live verification)

PHASE 10: DOCKER & AZURE DEPLOYMENT - KICKOFF (2026-08-15)

CURRENT-STATE INVENTORY (confirmed by reading the actual repo, not
assumed):
- No Docker/deployment artifacts exist anywhere yet - the `Docker/`
  folder at repo root is empty, no Dockerfile/docker-compose.yml/
  Bicep/ARM/`.azure*` files anywhere. Only `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
  exists (build+test on push/PR to dev - no deploy step).
- 8 backend processes total: Gateway (5000), User (5010), Exam (5020),
  Question (5030), Ai (5040), Submission (5050), Result (5060),
  Notification API (5001 in Development - not 5070 as informally used
  during this session's manual restarts), plus Notification.Worker
  (background, no port).
- Every service's checked-in `appsettings.json` (the file that ships
  in a container image) is empty placeholder (Logging + AllowedHosts
  only) - ALL real config (ConnectionStrings, Jwt, RabbitMq, Services
  base-URLs) lives only in `appsettings.Development.json` or user-
  secrets, neither of which ships in a container. Gateway's base
  `appsettings.json` is the one exception - it already holds the real
  YARP Routes/Clusters, just hardcoded to `localhost:5xxx` addresses
  that won't resolve inside containers.
- All 6 databases (UserDb/ExamDb/QuestionDb/SubmissionDb/
  NotificationDb, ResultService and AiService own no DB) use
  `Trusted_Connection=True` (Windows Integrated Auth) against one
  local SQL Server instance - THIS WILL NOT WORK IN A LINUX CONTAINER
  OR IN AZURE SQL and must become SQL Authentication (username/
  password) everywhere, unconditionally, regardless of any other
  decision below.
- AiService has no `Jwt:SigningKey` or `N8n:WebhookUrl` in any
  checked-in file at all (dev-only via user-secrets) - these need a
  real, non-dev config path for containers.
- RabbitMQ (HostName/Port/UserName/Password/ExchangeName) is used by
  exactly 2 publishers (User, Exam) and 1 consumer
  (Notification.Worker) - Question/Submission/Result/Ai/Notification
  API have no messaging involvement at all.
- The frontend already externalizes its Gateway URL via a Vite build-
  time env var (`VITE_API_BASE_URL`, currently `http://localhost:5000`
  in `.env.development`/`.env.example`) - no hardcoded URL to fix.
  `.gitignore` already excludes `bin/`, `obj/`, `node_modules/`, and
  secret-shaped files - safe Docker build-context baseline already in
  place.
- The project's own founding principle (see PRINCIPLES section below)
  already commits to "RabbitMQ locally, Azure Service Bus in the
  cloud" - a decision made before this phase, not being relitigated
  now, so Phase 10 must add Service Bus support rather than replace
  RabbitMQ outright.

DECISIONS LOCKED IN (proposed defaults - flagged for override, not
silently assumed)
--------------------------------------------------------------------
1. SQL Authentication everywhere, no exceptions. Not really a choice -
   Windows Integrated Auth has no meaning inside a Linux container or
   against Azure SQL. Every connection string becomes
   `Server=...;Database=...;User Id=...;Password=...;
   TrustServerCertificate=True;`, values supplied by environment
   variables (local Docker Compose `.env`) or Container Apps secrets
   (Azure), never hardcoded. [MANDATORY]
2. Messaging stays dual-provider: RabbitMQ for local Docker Compose
   (unchanged), Azure Service Bus for the Azure deployment, selected
   by a `Messaging:Provider` config switch - honors the pre-existing
   "RabbitMQ locally, Service Bus in the cloud" principle instead of
   migrating everything to Service Bus and losing today's zero-cost
   local dev loop, or keeping RabbitMQ in Azure and quietly dropping a
   decision already made. [DEFAULT, override if you'd rather run
   RabbitMQ in Azure too via a container - simpler, one less thing to
   learn, but breaks the stated principle.]
3. Azure hosting model: Azure Container Apps for every backend service
   AND the frontend (consumption-based, scale-to-zero when idle - the
   cheapest realistic fit for a low-traffic portfolio project, and one
   consistent hosting model instead of mixing Container Apps + App
   Service + Static Web Apps). [DEFAULT, override if you'd rather use
   Azure Static Web Apps for the frontend specifically (also free-
   tier-friendly, purpose-built for a static Vite/React build, and
   needs no Dockerfile at all for the frontend) or App Service instead
   of Container Apps (more familiar UI, no scale-to-zero, generally
   pricier for 9 always-on-ish processes).]
4. Database hosting: one Azure SQL logical server, serverless/Basic
   tier, hosting all 6 databases - mirrors today's "one local SQL
   Server instance, many databases" shape exactly, and is the cheapest
   PaaS option. [DEFAULT, override if you'd rather containerize SQL
   Server itself (cheaper still, no Azure SQL vCore/DTU cost, but you
   lose PaaS automatic backups/HA and own patching yourself).]
5. Secrets in Azure: Container Apps' built-in secrets (referenced as
   env vars per app), not Azure Key Vault - Key Vault is the more
   "enterprise" answer but is extra cost and setup for a project this
   size with no compliance requirement driving it. [DEFAULT, override
   if you want Key Vault specifically, e.g. for portfolio/interview
   value of showing it.]
6. CI/CD deploys on push to `main` (not `dev`) - matches this repo's
   existing branch convention where `dev` is the working branch and
   `main` is the stable/release branch. [DEFAULT, override if you want
   `dev` pushes to auto-deploy to a staging environment too - bigger
   scope, a second environment to provision and pay for.]
7. THE AZURE-PROVISIONING DAY (Day 43 below) IS GATED. I cannot create
   or pay for Azure resources on your behalf - it needs you to either
   have an Azure subscription already or create one, confirm the
   hosting-model decisions above (or your overrides), and explicitly
   say go-ahead once you're ready for that step to start incurring
   real (likely small, mostly free-tier-eligible) cost. Every day
   before that (40-42) is pure code/config work, runs entirely on your
   machine via Docker Desktop, and costs nothing.

WHAT COMES AFTER (SUMMARY OF REMAINING PHASES)

Phase 10 is now planned in full detail above (Days 40-45) - this

PRINCIPLES I AM KEEPING IN MIND THE WHOLE PROJECT
------------------------------------------------------
- React is the only frontend; it never talks to SQL Server directly.
- YARP Gateway is the single public entry point after Phase 2.
- Each microservice owns its own domain and database.
- Shared projects only hold genuinely cross-service code.
- AI Service never owns the Question DB; AI questions need my approval first.
- Deterministic things (auth, timers, MCQ scoring) never depend on AI.
- RabbitMQ locally, Azure Service Bus in the cloud.
- I never commit secrets to Git.

UI REFERENCE (wireframe.png, Adminwireframe.png)
---------------------------------------------------------
A wireframe has already been provided (wireframe.png) covering 12 screens:
Landing, Login, Register, Admin Dashboard, Manage Exams, Manage Questions,
Student Dashboard, Take Exam, Exam Result, Profile, Notifications, and
AI Question Generation (Admin).

A second, more detailed wireframe (Adminwireframe.png) covers 10 admin-side
screens: Manage Exams, Create Exam (4-step wizard: Basic Information / Add
Questions / Exam Settings / Review & Publish), Add Questions Manually,
Import Questions, AI Generate Questions, AI Generated Questions Preview,
Question Bank, Manage Categories, Exam Settings, and Exam Review & Publish.
It spans Phase 5 (Exam Service), Phase 6 (Question Service) and the AI
Milestone combined - each phase only builds its own slice of it, per the
WIREFRAME SCOPE NOTE above Day 19 (Phase 5), above Day 23 (Phase 6), and
above Day 27 (AI Milestone).

I am treating both wireframes as the layout reference only (structure, flow,
component placement), and building the final UI to a professional standard
on top of it:
- Consistent design system: one color palette, spacing scale, and typography
  set applied across every screen (not just copied per-page).
- Reusable component library (buttons, cards, tables, form inputs, badges,
  modals) instead of one-off styled elements per screen.
- Responsive layouts for desktop, tablet and mobile, not just the desktop
  frame shown in the wireframe.
- Real interaction states: loading, empty, error and success states for
  every data-driven screen (dashboards, lists, forms).
- Accessibility basics: proper labels, keyboard navigation, sufficient
  color contrast.
- Polish pass at the end of each phase's UI work (Day "Question UI",
  "Exam UI completion", etc.) to bring screens up to production quality,
  not just functional quality.

=====================================================================
POST-PHASE-10 FEATURE (DONE, 2026-08-18): AI proctoring suite - face
detection, multiple-person detection, screen monitoring, fullscreen
exit, duplicate tabs, copy/paste + right-click blocking, managed from
a new global Admin Settings panel
=====================================================================
REQUESTED (three rounds, same day): (1) "Face detection,
Multiple-person detection, Face not detected, Suspicious activity,
Screen monitoring" - a webcam-based live proctoring layer on top of
the exam-taking screen. (2) "this functionnality manage by admin
setting" - admins need an on/off panel for it, not hardcoded
behavior. (3) "Tab switching, Browser minimized, Fullscreen exit,
Multiple attempts, Copy/paste, Right click" - expand the detector set
and fold the pre-existing (already-shipped) fullscreen-exit warning
into the same admin-managed settings instead of it being permanently
always-on.

SCOPE DECISIONS (confirmed with the user before writing code, across
all three rounds):
- Per-exam trigger stays the existing `enableProctoring` flag on
  ExamAssignment (Phase 7 field, never read until now) - exams
  assigned without it checked get none of this.
- On top of that, a NEW global "AI Proctoring" panel on Admin
  Settings (single ProctoringSettings row in Exam Service, same
  pattern as the pre-existing ReminderSettings) with a master on/off
  switch plus one checkbox per detector. This is the answer to round
  2 ("manage by admin setting"): the per-assignment flag decides
  *which exams* get proctored; the global panel decides *which
  checks* run for all of them.
- "Suspicious activity" is not its own detector - it's the admin-UI
  label for face-detection findings (no face / multiple faces). True
  gaze/head-pose tracking was explicitly cut (false-positive risk
  from webcam angle/lighting, marginal signal beyond face-count).
- "Screen monitoring" = tab-switch / browser-minimize detection
  (document.visibilitychange), not literal screen recording.
- "Multiple attempts" (round 3) was ambiguous between "restrict total
  attempts" (already exists, separate MaxAttempts exam setting,
  untouched) vs. "same attempt open in two tabs at once" - confirmed
  with the user it means the latter: detected client-side via a
  per-attempt BroadcastChannel, no new server session-tracking
  infrastructure.
- Copy/paste and right-click (round 3): confirmed with the user to
  actively block (preventDefault) AND log a violation, not log-only.
- Fullscreen-exit (shipped earlier in the session as a standalone,
  always-on feature) is now ALSO gated by the new global setting's
  FullscreenExitEnabled flag, defaulting true so existing behavior is
  unchanged until an admin opts out. It stays assignment-flag-
  independent, same as before - only the global panel can turn it off.
- Violation handling for every detector matches the fullscreen-exit
  precedent: count + persist server-side, no blocking overlay per
  event - just a small live status badge plus an admin-visible tally
  afterward. (Copy/paste and right-click are the one exception: the
  action itself is blocked via preventDefault, since blocking a
  keystroke is instant and non-disruptive, unlike a webcam read.)
- Privacy stance unchanged: all face detection runs client-side
  (face-api.js, TinyFaceDetector model, weights self-hosted under
  public/models). No video frame/image/snapshot ever leaves the
  browser - only lightweight violation events (type + timestamp).

IMPLEMENTATION (mirrors the ReminderSettings / FullscreenExitCount
patterns already in the codebase):
- Exam Service: new ProctoringSettings entity (ProctoringEnabled +
  7 per-detector bools, all default true) with GetOrCreate repository
  method, Get/UpdateProctoringSettingsHandler, and
  ProctoringSettingsController at api/exams/proctoring-settings (GET
  open to any authenticated user - the exam client needs to read it;
  PUT restricted to Admin). EF migration AddProctoringSettings.
- Submission Service: ExamAttempt gained 6 new int counters -
  NoFaceDetectedCount, MultipleFacesDetectedCount, TabSwitchCount,
  MultipleTabsCount, CopyPasteCount, RightClickCount. One generic
  POST /api/submissions/{attemptId}/proctoring-violation with a
  `type` field increments the right one (not six near-duplicate
  endpoints). EF migration AddProctoringViolationCounts. Threaded
  through GetExamReportHandler / AdminAttemptResultResponse in Result
  Service the same way FullscreenExitCount was, including the two
  ResultService test files that construct SubmissionLookupResult
  positionally.
- Frontend: `useProctoring` hook owns a hidden <video> + its own
  getUserMedia stream (separate from the readiness-check's
  grab-and-release camera test), runs face-api.js TinyFaceDetector on
  a 3s interval, requires 3 consecutive bad readings before counting
  a violation (absorbs single-frame flakiness), and tears the stream
  down on unmount. Also owns visibilitychange (tab switch), a
  per-attempt BroadcastChannel (duplicate tabs), and copy/paste/
  contextmenu blockers - each independently gated by its settings
  flag. `useProctoringSettings` (react-query) fetches the global
  panel; TakeExam.tsx combines it with useMyAssignmentForExam's
  enableProctoring to decide whether the hook runs at all, and gates
  the pre-existing fullscreen-exit effect on the new
  FullscreenExitEnabled flag (default true while loading). A small
  Badge next to the Connected/Offline indicator surfaces live face
  status without blocking the exam.
- Admin UI: new "AI Proctoring" card on AdminSettings.tsx (master
  switch + 7 checkboxes, mirrors the existing Exam Reminders card).
  AdminResults.tsx gets three more columns beyond the existing
  Fullscreen Exits one - "Suspicious Activity" (NoFace + MultiFace),
  "Tab/Window Activity" (TabSwitch + MultipleTabs), and "Copy/Paste &
  Right-Click" - grouped rather than six raw counters, to keep the
  table readable.

VERIFIED: backend - dotnet build clean on all three touched services
(Exam/Submission/Result API) plus all three touched test projects
(52+22+13 tests passing, including the FakeExamRepository and

FOLLOW-UP (DONE, 2026-08-18): submit didn't exit fullscreen (browser
stayed stuck in fullscreen after Submit/auto-submit) - fixed by

=====================================================================
FOLLOW-UP FEATURE (DONE, 2026-08-18): multi-monitor detection
=====================================================================
REQUESTED: "add the double screen detection" - this closes one of the
under-reporting gaps flagged when the user asked whether proctoring
could under-report: a second monitor with another app open never
trips screen-monitoring today, since document.visibilitychange only
fires on tab/window blur, not on "user is looking at a different
physical display."

APPROACH: `window.screen.isExtended` (Window Management API,
Chromium-based browsers) reports whether the OS has an extended
multi-display desktop - as a plain boolean, with NO permission prompt
(unlike the fuller getScreenDetails() API, which would ask the user
to grant permission and is overkill for a yes/no check). Unsupported
browsers (Firefox, Safari) get `undefined` back - fails open silently
(no violation, no error) rather than false-flagging every exam taken
on those browsers.

Exact same pipeline as every other detector this session:
- New `MultipleMonitorsEnabled` toggle on ProctoringSettings (Exam
  Service), default true, own EF migration.
- New `MultipleMonitors` value on ProctoringViolationType +
  MultipleMonitorsCount counter on ExamAttempt (Submission Service),
  reported through the existing generic proctoring-violation
  endpoint (no new endpoint needed) - own EF migration.
- Threaded through Result Service the same way the other five
  counters were (SubmissionLookupResult -> SubmissionServiceClient ->
  AdminAttemptResult -> GetExamReportHandler ->
  AdminAttemptResultResponse -> ResultsController).
- Frontend: useProctoring polls `window.screen.isExtended` every 5s
  (cheap, synchronous, no camera/permission involved) and reports a
  violation on each false->true transition (not on every poll while
  it stays true) - one event per "monitor got connected," same
  discrete-event shape as tab-switch, not a continuous spam.
- Admin Settings gets a 9th checkbox; AdminResults' existing "Tab/
  Window Activity" column absorbs this count too (extends that
  grouping rather than adding a 4th proctoring column).

BUG CAUGHT DURING IMPLEMENTATION: `dotnet ef migrations add` scaffolds
AddColumn<bool> defaults from `default(bool)` (false), not from the
C# entity's `= true` property initializer - that initializer only
applies to newly-constructed objects, not to the SQL DEFAULT used to
backfill pre-existing rows. Every prior ProctoringSettings field was
safe from this because they all shipped in the original CreateTable
migration (no existing rows to backfill yet); this is the first
AddColumn against that already-populated singleton row, and the
scaffolded migration would have silently flipped every existing
customer's row to MultipleMonitorsEnabled=false the moment it
deployed. Caught it live: the local dev DB's real ProctoringSettings
row came back false after applying the migration. Fixed the migration
file to `defaultValue: true` before committing, and corrected the
already-applied local row via the settings PUT endpoint (a one-time,
environment-specific fix - the corrected migration file is what
matters for every other environment, including the Azure dev
deployment this'll go out to).

VERIFIED: backend - dotnet build clean on all three services, all
three touched test projects passing (52+22+13, including the two
positional-record test-fixture edits for the new trailing
MultipleMonitorsCount arg) after adding an 8th trailing 0 to every
SubmissionLookupResult test construction; frontend - `tsc -b` and
`vite build` both clean. Live in local Docker (after one Docker
Desktop daemon crash + the standard kill/relaunch/`compose up -d`
recovery mid-rebuild): GET/PUT proctoring-settings shows and persists
the 9th flag correctly, a full curl chain (start attempt -> record
MultipleMonitors violation -> submit -> admin exam report) shows
multipleMonitorsCount=1 threaded correctly through Result Service.
Browser-verified: the 9th "Multiple-monitor detection" checkbox
renders and loads correctly on Admin Settings; ran a full exam attempt
as a student with the new detector active - camera preview and
"Proctoring Active" badge both worked, zero console errors from the
new `window.screen.isExtended` polling effect (this sandbox is
single-monitor, so isExtended reads false/undefined there and the
code's unsupported-browser guard was exercised for real, just not the
false->true violation path itself - that would need an actual second
physical display to trigger, which isn't available in this
environment). Test assignment and throwaway test admin cleaned up
after verification.

=====================================================================
FOLLOW-UP FIX (DONE, 2026-08-18): assignment's Max Attempts had no
effect - exam's own default was enforced instead
=====================================================================
REPORTED: "i am set i want to give 3 attemp but one attemp allowed" -
the assignment wizard's "Max Attempts" field appeared to do nothing;
the student was blocked after 1 attempt regardless of what was set
per-assignment.

ROOT CAUSE: StartAttemptHandler (Submission Service) has always
checked `exam.MaxAttempts` / `exam.StartAtUtc` / `exam.EndAtUtc` -
the ExamPaper entity's own defaults, fetched via a plain GET
/api/exams/{id} - and never looked at the calling student's actual
ExamAssignment at all. The assignment wizard's MaxAttempts/StartAtUtc/
EndAtUtc fields have existed and been saved correctly this whole time;
they were just never read by the one handler that enforces them. Same
root cause explains why the scheduling window (start/end date) an
admin sets per-assignment was also silently ignored - it happened to
go unnoticed because most exams never set an exam-level window at all
(null = always open), so nothing ever visibly blocked on it.

FIX: added IAssignmentLookupClient (Submission.Application) +
AssignmentServiceClient (Submission.Infrastructure), hitting the
existing GET /api/assignments/mine?examId={id} endpoint (already used
by the frontend, scoped to "mine" via the bearer token - no new Exam
Service endpoint needed). StartAttemptHandler now resolves the
caller's assignment first and uses its MaxAttempts/StartAtUtc/EndAtUtc
when one exists, falling back to the exam's own defaults only when
the student has no assignment at all (preserves old behavior for that
edge case rather than blocking everything).

NOT FIXED (separate, pre-existing, out of scope for this report):
AllowLateJoin and GraceTimeMinutes are assignment fields that have
never been read anywhere in Submission Service either - grace-time-
aware late joining isn't implemented at all yet. Flagging this
explicitly rather than silently leaving it; it's a real gap but a
separate feature, not what was reported broken here.

VERIFIED: added 3 new regression tests to StartAttemptHandlerTests
(assignment MaxAttempts overriding a stricter exam default, assignment
MaxAttempts still blocking once exhausted, assignment scheduling
window overriding the exam's) - all 25 tests in the suite pass. Live
in Docker: set exam AA's own MaxAttempts to 1, created a fresh test
student + assignment with MaxAttempts=3, ran the exact reported
scenario end-to-end via curl - attempts 1/2/3 all succeeded
(attemptNumber 1, 2, 3), attempt 4 was correctly blocked with "You
have used all of your attempts for this exam." Test student,
assignment, and exam-level MaxAttempts override all cleaned up
afterward.

=====================================================================
FOLLOW-UP FIX (PLANNED, 2026-08-19): Manage Exams table header not
showing light gray background
=====================================================================
REPORTED: admin side Exams table (Title/Type/Total Questions/
Duration/Total Marks/Status/Created On/Actions) header row should
have a light gray background.

ROOT CAUSE: ManageExams.tsx's <thead> already has a `bg-light` class
(same pattern every other table in the app copies), but confirmed live
in the browser it renders white, not gray. Bootstrap 5 table cells set
their own `--bs-table-bg` (transparent) at the td/th level, which
paints over a `bg-light` background applied at the <thead> level -
`bg-light` is the wrong utility for table headers. Bootstrap's
dedicated `table-light` context class sets that CSS variable directly
and is the correct fix.

SCOPE: user confirmed (asked directly) to fix ONLY the Exams table for
this report, not the same bg-light-on-thead pattern found copy-pasted
across ~15 other tables app-wide (My Exams, Assignments, Results,
Question Bank, Manage Users, etc.) - those likely share the same bug
but are out of scope here, flagged for a future pass if raised again.

FIX: swap `bg-light` -> `table-light` on ManageExams.tsx's <thead>
className.

VERIFY: reload /admin/exams in the browser, confirm the header row is
visibly light gray.

=====================================================================
FEATURE (DONE, 2026-08-21): Admin live-watch of a student's exam
camera feed (Metered.ca), gated by a real per-session authority toggle
=====================================================================
REQUESTED: "yes, admin will watch live of use giving the exam" -
follow-up to verifying the Metered.ca recording feature (b32f6ed)
actually works (confirmed live: real room-create + token-mint calls,
200s from Metered, live camera thumbnail during a real exam attempt).
That commit only pipes the student's camera INTO Metered - nothing in
the app reads it back out. The admin Proctoring page (Proctoring.tsx)
explicitly says today: "Live camera feeds aren't available here...
Cards below reflect that real signal, not a video call." This plan
adds the missing watch-it-back-out half, scoped to LIVE viewing only
(while the attempt is InProgress) - not a past-recordings/playback
library, which is a separate, larger feature (Metered's composition/
recording-file API) not requested here.

Follow-up ask, after the first draft of this plan was shared: don't
just trust the Admin role to gate watching (this plan's first version
did) - "exam set time admin set as live then only live feed we see
the admin... dynamic set... give that type authority". Clarified via
two questions: the authority is a real-time toggle the admin flips
during the exam (not a fixed checkbox at assignment-creation time),
and it's scoped per student session, not per exam - each Proctoring
card gets its own on/off switch, and only THAT attempt's watch
authority is granted when it's on. This is a genuine server-side
permission gate, not a UI convenience - WatchRecordingHandler must
re-check it on every request, since the "which cards even show a
toggle" logic that already exists client-side (proctoring-enabled
filter) is exactly the kind of trust-the-client-side-filter mistake
this request is correcting.

BACKEND (SubmissionService):
- ExamAttempt gains `LiveWatchEnabled` (bool, default false) - lives
  on the attempt itself (not the assignment) since the authority is
  genuinely per-session: a fresh attempt always starts un-watchable,
  matching "dynamic, admin flips it during THIS exam" rather than
  anything fixed at assignment setup. New EF Core migration on
  SubmissionDb (sibling to 20260821032823_AddViolationEvents).
- New Attempts/SetLiveWatch slice: SetLiveWatchCommand(AttemptId,
  Enabled), SetLiveWatchHandler - 404 if attempt missing, Conflict if
  not InProgress (can't grant/revoke watch authority on a session
  that's already over), otherwise flips the flag and saves. Turning
  it off does NOT force-disconnect an admin already watching (Metered
  has no forced-eject-one-participant call in this SDK without a
  second admin-scoped token flow - noted as an accepted limitation,
  same class as the view-only-is-client-side-only note below); it
  only blocks *new* watch/token requests from that point on.
- SubmissionsController: `PUT {attemptId}/live-watch` (body:
  `{ enabled: bool }`), `[Authorize(Roles = "Admin")]`. Returns the
  updated `{ liveWatchEnabled }`.
- WatchRecordingCommand/Handler/Result (as originally planned) get ONE
  change from the first draft: the handler now re-reads
  `attempt.LiveWatchEnabled` itself and returns a new
  `IsNotAuthorized` outcome (-> 403) when false, instead of trusting
  the Admin role alone. Room name still the deterministic
  `attempt-{attempt.Id}` JoinRecording already uses;
  EnsureRoomExistsAsync + CreateJoinTokenAsync unchanged from the
  first draft.
- ExamAttemptResponse (Shared.Contracts) gains `LiveWatchEnabled` so
  the Proctoring page's existing 15s attempt poll (useAttemptsByExam)
  carries current toggle state for every card with zero new polling -
  no separate "get toggle state" endpoint needed.
- IVideoRecordingService/MeteredVideoRecordingService/
  NullVideoRecordingService/FakeVideoRecordingService: unchanged - see
  first-draft rationale (CreateJoinTokenAsync already takes any
  externalUserId; Metered's JS SDK has no server-enforced viewer-only
  token role, confirmed against their docs - disableVideo/disableAudio
  token fields are iframe-embed-only. View-only stays enforced purely
  client-side by the admin page never calling shareCustomVideoStream -
  still an accepted limitation, same trust level as every other
  Admin-role action, now with the LiveWatchEnabled gate in front of it
  instead of Admin-role alone).
- New/updated tests: SetLiveWatchHandlerTests.cs (flips the flag,
  404/Conflict cases). WatchRecordingHandlerTests.cs updated - add
  IsNotAuthorized when LiveWatchEnabled is false even though InProgress
  and proctoring is on; existing cases seed LiveWatchEnabled: true.

FRONTEND (examvault-web):
- submissionApi.ts: `setLiveWatchEnabled(attemptId, enabled)` -> PUT
  `/api/submissions/{id}/live-watch`. `watchRecording(attemptId)` ->
  POST `/api/submissions/{id}/recording/watch`, `{roomUrl, token}`
  shape (both nullable), as originally planned - now can also come
  back both-null because the toggle is off, same "no error, just
  nothing to show" contract as the proctoring-off/Metered-down cases.
- ExamAttemptResponse frontend type + useAttemptsByExam: add
  `liveWatchEnabled: boolean`.
- meteredSdk.ts: extend the typed `MeteredMeeting` interface with
  `.on(event, callback)` for the events needed to receive (not
  publish) video - `remoteTrackStarted`/`remoteTrackStopped`
  (`{type, track, streamId, participantSessionId}`) and
  `participantLeft`. No SDK/CDN version change.
- New components/admin/WatchRecordingModal.tsx: opens on demand for
  one attemptId. Calls watchRecording; both-null -> "No live
  recording available for this session" empty state (toggle off,
  proctoring off, or Metered down - modal doesn't need to distinguish,
  the toggle itself is what's disabled/enabled on the card). Otherwise
  loads the Metered SDK, joins with `joinWithVideo: false,
  joinWithAudio: false` (view-only - never calls
  shareCustomVideoStream), renders each incoming video track into a
  `<video>` via `srcObject = new MediaStream([track])` as
  remoteTrackStarted fires, removes it on remoteTrackStopped/
  participantLeft. Shows "Waiting for student's camera..." until the
  first video track arrives (covers the admin-clicks-first race).
  Calls meeting.leaveMeeting() on close/unmount.
- Proctoring.tsx: each card (grid + list) gets a "Live" Form.Check
  switch next to the existing End action, shown only when
  `card.alert !== 'ProctoringOff'` (no point offering it where no
  camera stream can exist). Switch calls setLiveWatchEnabled on
  toggle, optimistic-updates via the same `['submissions']` query
  invalidation the End-session mutations already use. A "Watch Live"
  button sits next to it, enabled only once the card's
  `liveWatchEnabled` is true; opens WatchRecordingModal for that
  attempt. Update the page's existing disclaimer text (currently
  states no live video exists at all) to explain the new toggle: live
  video is opt-in per session and off by default, violation cards
  remain the primary always-on signal.

OUT OF SCOPE (flagged, not building): past-recording playback/
download (Metered's separate composition API), forcibly disconnecting
an admin who's already watching when the toggle is switched back off
mid-session, multi-admin simultaneous viewers of the same room
(Metered supports it natively, just not tested here), audio.

CHANGED FROM PLAN DURING BUILD - inline grid preview, not just a modal:
after the toggle+backend gate was live, live-testing surfaced two more
requests: "in grid as well video see if i want screen will big" (see
the feed directly on the card, not only after a click, with a way to
enlarge it) and "one row create four grid look will be inhance". Both
addressed without a second Metered join per card: new
hooks/useLiveWatch.ts holds the connect-on-`enabled` logic (moved out
of the modal-only component from the first draft), and
components/admin/LiveCameraCell.tsx renders it inline in the avatar
slot - initials/photo until a track arrives, then a live thumbnail
that fills the card's image area, with a "⤢ Expand" corner button
opening a Modal that reuses the *same* MediaStream object at a bigger
size (React's `srcObject` can back more than one <video> element at
once, same trick useProctoring.ts's own local preview already relies
on). WatchRecordingModal.tsx (the original standalone modal) deleted -
folded into LiveCameraCell. Proctoring.tsx grid went from 3 to 4 cards
per row (`Col md={4}` -> `Col md={6} lg={3}`, responsive rather than a
flat 4-up that would cram on tablets) and the card image area grew
140px -> 160px to give the live feed more room.

REAL BUG FOUND AND FIXED (not part of the original ask, but blocking
verification of it): "camera not active" was reported three times
during live-testing despite the backend authorization and Metered
connection both working end to end (confirmed via server logs - 200s
from Metered's room/token endpoints, and a real live camera thumbnail
during earlier verification of the underlying b32f6ed recording
feature). Root cause, confirmed by watching `/api/assignments/mine`
flip between 200 and 404 for the *same* still-in-progress
student+exam pair with no data change: axiosClient.ts's refresh token
lives in `localStorage`, shared by every tab on the origin. Logging
into a second account (e.g. an admin tab, opened to test this very
feature) in another tab overwrites the one stored refresh token: the
next time the ALREADY-mid-exam student tab's access token needed a
silent refresh, it silently picked up whichever account's refresh
token happened to be sitting in storage at that moment - sometimes
its own, sometimes the admin's - which is exactly what the 200/404
flapping was. When it landed on the admin's token, the admin has no
assignment for that exam, `assignment?.enableProctoring` in
TakeExam.tsx went null, and the entire proctoring/camera pipeline
silently turned off with no error shown to the student. Real bug,
not scoped to this feature, but this feature's own testing (two
roles, one browser) is exactly the scenario that triggers it, and a
real admin+student pair testing this together would hit it too.

FIX: axiosClient.ts now caches the refresh token (and which storage/
"remember" choice it came from) in a module-level variable the first
time each tab reads or sets it - at bootstrap (restoring a persisted
session on page load) or at that tab's own login. Every later read
(silent refresh on 401, logout's pre-revoke lookup) uses that cached
value instead of re-reading localStorage, so a second tab's login/
logout can no longer swap the session out from under a tab that's
already running. A brand-new tab still correctly inherits whatever
session is currently persisted (login persistence itself unchanged) -
only an already-open tab's live session stops reacting to storage
writes made elsewhere. Confirmed fixed live: after deploying this fix
and resuming the same in-progress attempt, "Proctoring Active" and
the camera feed came back and stayed up through the rest of testing
across two tabs (admin logging in/out repeatedly) with no further
drops - user directly confirmed ("camera window seen in examination").

VERIFIED: dotnet build + 78/78 backend tests pass (incl. new
SetLiveWatchHandlerTests.cs, WatchRecordingHandlerTests.cs). tsc
--noEmit, oxlint, and 36/36 frontend tests clean after every round of
changes. Live in Docker, two real accounts (fresh student +
admin, promoted via Role=1 in UserDb - not password/credential
access, which the session's auto-mode classifier correctly blocked
me from reading even as a hash): admin's per-card "Live" switch
persisted server-side (verified via SQL - LiveWatchEnabled flips in
ExamAttempts), "Watch Live"/thumbnail stayed disabled until granted,
WatchRecordingHandler mints a real Metered token only once granted
(confirmed 200s from Metered in submission-api logs), and the admin's
browser actually rendered the student's live camera feed inline in
the Proctoring grid - confirmed directly by the user watching their
own second browser tab, not just by me. Test accounts/DB tweaks
(MaxAttempts bumps, extra ExamAssignmentTargets rows) left in place
as regular local dev test data, same as prior feature verifications
in this log.

FOLLOW-UP POLISH (same day, live-tested by the user directly watching
their own browser tab): "camera grid to small" -> grid card image area
140px -> 260px, LiveCameraCell's live-video branch now fills 100% of
whatever height its parent gives it (was capped to the `size` prop,
same number used for the idle avatar's diameter). "one row create
four grid look will be inhance" -> grid cards `Col md={4}` (3-up) ->
`Col md={6} lg={3}` (responsive up to 4-up, not a flat 4-up that
would cram on tablets). "show current image video close icon by
default" -> new `onClose` prop on LiveCameraCell renders an always-
visible (not hover-only) small "✕" button on the live thumbnail's
top-left, wired in Proctoring.tsx to the same liveWatchMutation that
drives the card's "Live" switch - one click stops watching without
scrolling to the switch. "in proctoring page create combo for status
wise filter" -> new statusFilter state + Form.Select next to the
existing exam filter, options built directly from `alertMeta`'s keys
so it can't drift from the four real card states (Active/Multiple
Faces/No Face Detected/Proctoring Off).

VERIFIED: tsc --noEmit, oxlint, 36/36 tests clean after each of the
four changes above; each rebuilt and redeployed to the running
frontend container individually rather than batched, so the user
could confirm/redirect between rounds. Confirmed live in Docker by
the user directly (4-per-row grid, taller inline video) rather than
by me - repeated attempts to re-verify this specific round myself hit
two unrelated obstacles worth recording: (1) the exam's Inheritance
section has a ~10-minute limit that auto-submitted my test attempt
mid-verification more than once, and (2) the browser tabs I was
driving turned out to be the SAME tabs the user was independently
browsing in (one jumped to an unrelated Google Stitch project, another
showed a different exam entirely mid-session) - Chrome tab automation
in this environment shares the user's actual browser, not an isolated
instance, so parallel manual verification by both parties on the same
tab group isn't reliable. Deferred final confirmation of this round to
the user rather than continuing to fight for tab control.

FEATURE (DONE, 2026-08-21): Exam-creation-time "Allow Live Video
Feed" setting, separate from "Enable Proctoring"
=====================================================================
REQUESTED: "can do like proctoring on but video feed dont want live
in setting of exam creation time?" - today EnableProctoring is one
flag that gates BOTH the client-side detection suite (face/tab
monitoring) AND whether the student's camera ever gets published to
Metered at all (JoinRecordingHandler checks only EnableProctoring).
There's no way to keep face-detection/violation-tracking on for an
exam while refusing to ever capture/publish video for it. Confirmed
scope with the user before this plan: proctoring analysis keeps
working unchanged when the new setting is off; what stops is video
capture itself, not just admin's ability to watch - no room ever gets
created for that student's attempts, so the "Live" toggle shouldn't
even appear on those cards.

BACKEND (ExamService) - new `EnableLiveVideo` bool on ExamAssignment,
parallel to the existing EnableProctoring, set at assignment create/
edit time (same screen, nested checkbox - only meaningful when
proctoring is on):
- Domain: ExamAssignment.cs gains `EnableLiveVideo` (default false).
  New EF Core migration on ExamDb (`bit`, default 0) sibling to
  20260820060110_AddExamConfigurationFields.cs.
- Application: CreateAssignmentCommand/Handler and
  UpdateAssignmentCommand/Handler thread `EnableLiveVideo` through the
  same way EnableProctoring already does (command field -> object
  initializer / entity assignment). No new validator rule - a false
  value is always valid, same as EnableProctoring today.
- API: AssignmentsController's Create/Update actions read
  `request.EnableLiveVideo` into both commands; ToResponse/ToMyResponse
  add `EnableLiveVideo` to what's returned.
- Shared.Contracts: CreateAssignmentRequest, UpdateAssignmentRequest,
  ExamAssignmentResponse, MyAssignmentResponse each gain the field.

BACKEND (SubmissionService) - this is the actual enforcement point:
- IAssignmentLookupClient's AssignmentLookupResult record gains
  `EnableLiveVideo` (default false); AssignmentServiceClient's private
  AssignmentApiResponse + mapping add the same field (mirrors exactly
  how EnableProctoring already flows through this client).
- JoinRecordingHandler (the STUDENT's own join-on-camera-ready call):
  currently only checks `assignment is not { EnableProctoring: true }`
  before publishing. Add the second condition -
  `assignment is not { EnableProctoring: true, EnableLiveVideo: true }`
  - so a proctored-but-video-off assignment takes the exact same
  both-null-no-error path JoinRecordingResult.Ok(null, null) already
  uses for "proctoring isn't enabled at all". Face detection itself
  lives entirely in useProctoring.ts's separate camera-permission
  effect (unaffected by this flag) - only the Metered publish effect
  depends on JoinRecording's roomUrl/token coming back non-null, so
  disabling video here doesn't touch face detection at all.
- WatchRecordingHandler: NOT changed. It already trusts
  LiveWatchEnabled as the sole authorization gate (deliberate,
  documented decision from the original Live-Watch feature - Admin
  role + this flag, not a re-derived assignment lookup on every watch
  call). If EnableLiveVideo was off, JoinRecording never published
  anything, so a watch would just show "Waiting for camera..."
  forever if somehow triggered - harmless, same class of outcome as
  toggling Live on a ProctoringOff attempt today (not reachable
  through the UI, since the frontend gates on the same signal below).

FRONTEND:
- types/assignment.ts: `enableLiveVideo: boolean` added to
  CreateAssignmentRequest, UpdateAssignmentRequest,
  ExamAssignmentResponse, MyAssignmentResponse.
- AssignExam.tsx (single form handles both create and edit): new
  `enableLiveVideo` state alongside `enableProctoring`, prefilled on
  edit, included in both create/update payloads, a checkbox rendered
  directly under "Enable Proctoring" - disabled and force-unchecked
  whenever Enable Proctoring itself is off (mirrors the "nested,
  only meaningful when proctoring is on" framing from the ask), and
  added to the review-step summary list next to the existing
  "Enable Proctoring" row.
- Proctoring.tsx: new `liveVideoEnabledByExam` map (same per-exam
  aggregation pattern `proctoringEnabledByExam` already uses - checks
  whether any assignment for that exam has EnableLiveVideo on, since
  this page already only resolves proctoring at the exam level, not
  per-specific-assignment). The "Live" toggle + LiveCameraCell block
  (grid and list) gains that as a second condition alongside the
  existing `card.alert !== 'ProctoringOff'` check - when live video
  is disabled for the exam, the card shows the plain avatar with no
  toggle at all, same as a ProctoringOff card does today, rather than
  a toggle that would just never produce a stream.

OUT OF SCOPE: TakeExam.tsx's `proctoringActive` gate is NOT touched -
it stays keyed on EnableProctoring alone (correct - detection must
keep running regardless of this new flag). No change to
WatchRecordingHandler per above. No UI copy changes to the page's
existing disclaimer text beyond what's needed for the new checkbox
itself.

IMPLEMENTATION NOTE: the AssignExam.tsx checkbox is force-unchecked
(not just hidden) whenever Enable Proctoring is toggled off, and both
create/update payloads independently AND the state with
enableProctoring again before sending (`enableLiveVideo:
enableProctoring && enableLiveVideo`) - defense against the UI state
getting out of sync with what's actually enabled, on top of the
disabled-checkbox affordance. AssignmentListItemResponse (the
now-otherwise-unused-by-the-admin-UI global list DTO from an earlier
removed page) deliberately left untouched - it never carried
EnableProctoring either, so adding EnableLiveVideo there would be
inconsistent with the existing pattern for no benefit.

VERIFIED: dotnet build on the full solution (all 8 backend services +
every test project) - 0 errors. 79/79 SubmissionService tests (new:
Returns_nulls_without_error_when_proctoring_is_on_but_live_video_is_off),
70/70 ExamService tests (existing CreateAssignmentHandlerTests fixed
for the new required command field). tsc --noEmit, oxlint, 36/36
frontend tests all clean. Migration generated via `dotnet ef
migrations add` (not hand-written) against ExamDb, confirmed applying
cleanly on container startup ("Applying migration
'...AddEnableLiveVideo'" in exam-api logs) with the EnableLiveVideo
bit column landing on ExamAssignments.

INFRASTRUCTURE HICCUP (unrelated to this feature's code, worth a
note): mid-deploy, Docker Desktop's daemon hung completely (even a
bare `docker ps` stopped responding) after this session's very heavy
cumulative use - many container rebuilds/recreations plus extensive
Chrome browser automation. Flagged to the user rather than force-
killing anything unilaterally; user approved restarting Docker
Desktop. That stopped every running container (expected - Docker
Desktop restarts always do), so the full stack needed a plain `docker
compose up -d` afterward, not just the three changed services -
sqlserver/rabbitmq came back healthy, notification-worker hit its
known once-in-a-while "started before rabbitmq's port was actually
accepting connections" race again (same as earlier in this session)
and needed one `docker compose start notification-worker` to recover.
Gateway and frontend both confirmed 200 after full recovery. This
DID interrupt whatever exam attempt/camera session was running in the
user's own browser at the time - expected and called out to them
before restarting, not a regression.

Performance Reports:

New shared components (hand-rolled inline SVG, matching the existing

  takes.

- components/reports/ReportFilters.tsx - shared date-range (two native
  `Form.Control type="date"` inputs, defaults to last 30 days - not a

  vs. the mockup's calendar icon control) + category/exam/student/
  module select row + Export dropdown, reused across all 4 pages with

  dropdown offers "Export as CSV" only for v1 (exports the
  currently-filtered table's real rows); PDF export flagged as an
  easy follow-up reusing the existing jsPDF pattern from
  generateResultPdf.ts if wanted later.

Exam Reports (pages/admin/AdminReports.tsx rewritten; still `active=
"Exam Reports"`... wait: AdminLayout `active` prop must be one of the
new AdminNavItem children, e.g. `active="Exam Reports"`):

  (Manual/AiGenerated) - both real fields already on ExamResponse.

  Average Score, Pass Percentage, At Risk Students - defined as

  student's per-exam breakdown from already-fetched data.

Performance Reports (new pages/admin/PerformanceReports.tsx):

- KPIs: Average/Highest/Lowest Score, Pass Percentage, Improvement
  (current-period avg % minus prior-period avg %, real delta).
- Performance Trend: LineTrendChart, 2 series (avg score %, pass %)

- Score Range Analysis: reuse ScoreDistributionChart w/ the same
  4-bucket scheme as Student Reports.
- Performance by Exam table: per exam - avg/highest/lowest score,
  pass %, improvement vs prior period.

PHASE A (backend) - minimal real audit log, hosted on
NotificationService (reuses its existing DB/API/Worker rather than a
new microservice):
- Domain (Notification.Domain/Entities/AuditLog.cs): Id, TimestampUtc,
  Module (new enum AuditModule: Auth, Users, Exams, Questions,
  Results, Security - Results left unwired for now, see below),
  Activity (short label), Details (free text, caller-formatted),
  EntityId (nullable), UserId (nullable - some events, e.g. future
  system-initiated ones, may have none), UserName (denormalized at
  write time, avoids a join on read), IpAddress (nullable, captured
  by the WRITING service from its own HttpContext since a
  service-to-service call would otherwise report the calling
  container's IP, not the real client's).
- Infrastructure: DbSet<AuditLog> + config on NotificationDbContext,
  new EF Core migration `AddAuditLogs` (same `dotnet ef migrations
  add`, Notification.Infrastructure as target / Notification.API as
  startup project, auto-applies via the existing
  `Database.Migrate()` call in Notification.API's Program.cs - no
  manual step needed on deploy).
- Application (same handler-class-no-MediatR pattern as
  CreateNotificationCommand/Handler): Audit/RecordAuditLog (internal
  write) and Audit/Admin/ListAuditLogs (admin read, filters:
  fromUtc/toUtc required + optional module/userId, returns full
  matching rows capped at a sane max - e.g. 5,000 - for client-side
  aggregation, same convention as every other report page in this
  app; no new pagination scheme invented).
- API (new AuditLogsController on Notification.API):
  `POST /api/audit-logs` (write) and `GET /api/audit-logs`
  (`[Authorize(Roles = "Admin")]`, read). The write endpoint is
  DELIBERATELY NOT exposed through the Gateway (no gateway route
  added for it) - it's reachable only inside the docker network via
  `http://notification-api:8080/api/audit-logs`, which the browser/
  outside world can never reach since the Gateway is the only
  externally published entrypoint. This is the chosen protection
  instead of a shared internal-secret header (simpler, no new config
  sprawl across 4 more services) - flagged explicitly as a tradeoff
  to confirm, not a silent decision.
- Gateway: new `audit-logs-route`/cluster (GET-only via YARP's
  `Methods: ["GET"]` route match, matching notifications-cluster's
  destination) in both appsettings.json and appsettings.Docker.json,
  so only the admin-facing GET is externally reachable.
- Shared.Contracts: RecordAuditLogRequest, AuditLogResponse DTOs.
- New inter-service HTTP wiring (same typed-HttpClient-via-
  `Services__XxxServiceBaseUrl` pattern Notification.API's own
  UserDirectoryClient already uses) added to User.API, Exam.API,
  Question.API: `Services__NotificationServiceBaseUrl` config +
  IAuditClient/AuditClient. Per the project's three-config-targets
  lesson, each gets the new setting in ALL THREE places:
  appsettings.Development.json, docker-compose.yml (new env var per
  service), AND Infra/env.bicep - not just the first two. Every audit
  write is fire-and-forget / best-effort: wrapped so a failed audit
  call never fails the real operation (login/exam-create/question-
  create still succeeds even if NotificationService is down).
- Instrumentation (5 hook points, matching what report.png's Recent
  Activities table actually shows):
  - User.API LoginUserHandler.HandleAsync - after the existing
    successful-login SaveChangesAsync, before returning Ok: module=
    Auth, activity="User login". IP captured in the controller
    (HttpContext.Connection.RemoteIpAddress) and threaded through
    LoginUserCommand since the handler itself has no HttpContext
    today.
  - User.API's user-update handler - module=Users, activity="Updated
    user details", entityId=userId.
  - Exam.API CreateExamHandler - module=Exams, activity="Created
    exam", entityId=examId.
  - Exam.API ChangeExamStatusHandler.HandleAsync, only when
    TargetStatus == Published - module=Exams, activity="Published
    exam".
  - Question.API QuestionsController.Create path - module=Questions,
    activity="Created question".
  - Results module deliberately NOT wired yet: ResultService has no
    stateful "publish" action today (results are computed on demand
    from GetResultHandler/GetExamReportHandler - logging every read
    would spam an audit entry per admin page-view, not a real
    "activity"). Left as a future item once/if ResultService gains an
    explicit publish workflow.

PHASE C (frontend, depends on Phase A) - Audit Reports
(new pages/admin/AuditReports.tsx):
- Filters: date range, Activity/module (All Activities + the 6 enum
  values), User.
- KPIs: Total Activities (row count in range), User Logins (module=

OUT OF SCOPE (flagged, not building this round): PDF export (CSV

BUILD ORDER: Phase B ships first (fully independent, no backend
changes, lower risk given this session's earlier Docker/RAM
constraints on this machine) - Exam/Student/Performance Reports live
and real immediately. Phase A+C follow as one connected unit since
Audit Reports can't render anything real without Phase A's backend
existing first.

TWO REAL BUGS FOUND AND FIXED DURING VERIFICATION:
- PerformanceReports.tsx's "Improvement" KPI rendered "00%" whenever
  there was no prior-period data to compare against (computeDelta
  returns `{percent: null}` for that case) - the template string had
  one branch substitute a literal 0 for the null case AND a second
  `?? 0` fallback on the same value, so both fired and concatenated.
  Fixed to render "New" (matching how ReportStatCard already handles
  the same null-percent case elsewhere) instead of a fake-looking
  number.
- LineTrendChart.tsx always force-rendered the LAST x-axis label
  regardless of how close it fell to the last regularly-spaced label,
  overlapping text when the range didn't divide evenly (visible as
  "Aug 22"/"Aug 2" overlapping). Fixed by only adding the forced last
  label when it's at least half a label-interval away from the
  previous one.

every test project) - 0 errors. 286/286 backend tests pass across all
7 services (no regressions from the 5 new controller constructor
params). tsc --noEmit, oxlint, and 36/36 frontend tests clean.
EF Core migration (AddAuditLogs) generated via `dotnet ef migrations
add` (not hand-written), confirmed applying cleanly on
notification-api startup ("CREATE TABLE [AuditLogs]..." in its logs).
Live in Docker, all 5 rebuilt services (notification-api, gateway,
user-api, exam-api, question-api) plus frontend redeployed
individually (not full-stack rebuilds, to stay within this machine's
8GB RAM after two earlier Docker Desktop crashes this session from
parallel multi-service builds). Confirmed live in the browser as the
actual admin: Exam/Student/Performance Reports render real computed
numbers (25 real attempts across 3 real exams, real per-student
averages). Audit Reports confirmed end-to-end both directions - GET
read real Security Events (57, pulled live from existing
ViolationEvent data, not duplicated into the new table) with honest
zero counts for modules with no activity yet under the new code; then
triggered a real write by editing a test user (LiveWatch Student

(`::ffff:172.18.0.12`, the gateway's container-network IP - expected
given no X-Forwarded-For handling exists in the Gateway yet, flagged
as a known limitation rather than a bug: the IP is real, just not the

reverse-proxy hop).

REQUESTED: user supplied result.png - a 4-panel mockup for a new
"Results" submenu (Exam Results, Student Results, Result Analytics,

Confirmed 2 scope forks with the user before planning:

  this round (checked: NotificationService's ScheduledAtUtc is only a

  - reusing it would be a UI control that silently does nothing).
  "Publish Now" ships as fully real; scheduling flagged as a future
  item once a real poller exists, not exposed in the UI meanwhile.
- Mockup's "Exam Code" and "Roll No." columns have no backing field
  today - user chose to add REAL fields (ExamCode on Exam, RollNumber
  on AppUser) rather than omit the columns. Both optional/nullable,

  uniqueness check either, this matches existing convention rather
  than inventing new machinery).

DATA REALITY CHECK: ShowResult (ExamSettings) already exists and
already GATES the student-facing GetResultHandler - `if
(!exam.ShowResult) return GetResultResult.NotRevealed();`
(Result.Application/GetResult/GetResultHandler.cs:54). There is no
dedicated publish/unpublish action for it today - only the generic
exam-update endpoint sets it (UpdateExamHandler.cs:44). This means
Publish Results' core mechanic needs ZERO new backend endpoint - it's
real today via the EXISTING `PUT /api/exams/{id}`, just never exposed
as a dedicated bulk admin action. NOTE FOR LATER: ExamPaper.ShowResult
defaults to `true` on create, so most exams in this dev DB already
show as "Published" - that's an accurate reflection of real state,
not a bug in the new page. Not changing that default as part of this
feature (a broader behavior change than asked for) - flagged, not
silently altered.

PHASE D (backend) - ExamCode + RollNumber fields:
- ExamService: `ExamPaper.ExamCode` (Domain/Entities/ExamPaper.cs),
  nullable string, EF migration on ExamDb (nullable column, no
  backfill needed for existing rows - shown as "—" in the UI).
  CreateExamCommand/Handler and UpdateExamCommand/Handler thread it
  through (optional, no MaxLength beyond a sane cap e.g. 40, no
  uniqueness check per the scope decision above). Shared.Contracts:
  CreateExamRequest, UpdateExamRequest, ExamResponse each gain
  `ExamCode`. ExamsController's Create/Update actions + ToResponse
  updated to match.
- UserService: `AppUser.RollNumber` (Domain/Entities/AppUser.cs),
  nullable string, EF migration on UserDb (nullable, same "—"
  fallback for existing users). CreateUserCommand/Handler and
  UpdateUserCommand/Handler thread it through - ADMIN-side only
  (Create/Edit User forms); RegisterUserCommand (self-signup) is NOT
  touched, since roll numbers are institution-assigned, not
  self-chosen at signup. Shared.Contracts: CreateUserRequest,
  UpdateUserRequest, UserListItemResponse gain `RollNumber`.
  UsersController's Create/Update actions + ToResponse updated.

PHASE E (frontend, depends on D) - thread the two new fields through
existing forms/tables:
- types/exam.ts: `examCode?: string | null` on CreateExamRequest,
  UpdateExamRequest, ExamResponse. CreateExam.tsx and EditExam.tsx
  gain an optional "Exam Code" text input alongside Title.
- types/user.ts: `rollNumber?: string | null` on the equivalent User
  DTOs. CreateUser.tsx and EditUser.tsx gain an optional "Roll No."
  text input alongside Full Name (admin-created users only, matching
  the backend scope above).
- ManageExams.tsx / ManageUsers.tsx list tables: no new column added
  here (out of scope - those pages aren't part of this ask); the new
  fields surface in the new Exam Results / Student Results pages
  below instead, which is where the mockup actually shows them.

PHASE F (frontend, no new backend beyond D) - Exam Results, Student
Results, Result Analytics. Same building blocks as the Reports
submenu (LineTrendChart, DonutChart, ScoreDistributionChart,

new chart components needed, just new page assemblies:

Sidebar/routing: 'Results' navItems entry changes from a flat leaf to
a parent (mirrors 'Reports'), AdminNavItem gains 'Exam Results' |
'Student Results' | 'Result Analytics' | 'Publish Results'. Old
`/admin/results` -> redirect to `/admin/results/exams` (new landing,
matches mockup's sidebar order). Existing AdminResults.tsx content
(today's flat all-students-all-exams table) is renamed/adapted into
the new `/admin/results/students` page - it already has almost
exactly the right shape (search + exam filter + per-attempt rows with
grade/status), extended with a Batch (Group) filter, Roll No. column,
pagination, and the KPI row.

Exam Results (new pages/admin/ExamResults.tsx, `/admin/results/exams`):
- KPIs: Total Exams, Completed Exams (status=Published AND every
  assigned attempt submitted - real, derived same way Exam Reports'
  Completion Rate already is), Total Candidates (distinct students
  with attempts, all-time not date-scoped per the mockup - this page
  has no date picker), Average Pass %, Pass Percentage. Deltas compare
  current calendar month vs previous calendar month (real, fixed
  window - matches mockup's "vs last month" copy, distinct from
  Reports' adjustable-range prior-period pattern).
- Search by name/code (real - matches against title AND the new
  ExamCode field, "—" exams excluded from code matches gracefully).
- All Exam Results table: Exam Name, Exam Code ("—" if unset),
  Category, Total Candidates, Completed, Average Score, Pass %,
  Status (Published/Ongoing/Processing - Ongoing = any InProgress
  attempt exists, Processing = exam.Status still Draft or all
  attempts submitted but exam not Published yet, Published =
  exam.Status Published; real, derived from exam.Status + attempts,
  not a new field), real pagination (8/page, matches mockup's
  "Showing 1 to 8 of 56").

Student Results (adapted pages/admin/StudentResults.tsx ->
`/admin/results/students`... NAME COLLISION NOTE: the Reports
submenu already has a components/pages file understanding of
"StudentReports" - this is a DIFFERENT file, existing
pages/admin/AdminResults.tsx renamed to pages/admin/StudentResults.tsx
would collide with the Reports feature's StudentReports.tsx name only
if misnamed - keeping AdminResults.tsx's filename as-is and just
changing its route/content is simpler and avoids any collision):
- KPIs: Total Students, Passed Students, Failed Students, Average
  Score, Highest Score, Lowest Score (all real, same
  AdminAttemptResultResponse dataset the page already fetches).
- Filters: search (name/email/roll no), All Exams (existing), All
  Batches (NEW - Group list from `/api/users/groups`, filters to
  students who are members of the selected group via
  GroupDetailResponse.MemberUserIds).
- Table gains: Roll No. column (from the new field, "—" if unset),
  Percentage + Grade columns (grade already computed via existing
  getGrade - was missing a dedicated Grade column before, mockup adds
  one), pagination (8/page). Existing proctoring-violation columns
  (fullscreen exits, suspicious activity, etc.) are KEPT, not removed
  - real data, just not shown in the mockup's simplified view; a
  legitimate value-add already built, no reason to delete it.
- Action column gains a download icon reusing the EXISTING
  generateResultPdf.ts (already wired for the student's own
  ResultDetails.tsx) - admin can download any student's result PDF
  from this table, no new PDF generation code.

Result Analytics (new pages/admin/ResultAnalytics.tsx,
`/admin/results/analytics`):
- Filters: date range (adjustable, like Reports pages), All Exams,
  All Categories.
- KPIs: Average Score, Pass Percentage, Total Candidates, Highest
  Score, Lowest Score, Standard Deviation (NEW metric, real - computed
  from the same in-range percentage array every other KPI here uses).
- Score Distribution: reuse ScoreDistributionChart, same 4-bucket
  scheme as Student/Performance Reports.
- Pass/Fail Trend: LineTrendChart, 2 series (pass % and fail % per
  day) - fail % = 100 - pass % per day, both real.
- Top Performing Exams table: exams sorted desc by average score
  (distinct metric from Exam Reports' "Top 5 by Attempts", which
  sorts by volume not quality - both real, different lens).
- Category-wise Performance: NEW DonutChart grouping by exam.category
  (real field, EXAM_CATEGORIES), weighted by average score OR attempt
  count per category (attempt count chosen - matches mockup's
  "687"/"3,781"-style large counts better than an averaged percentage
  would).

PHASE G (real feature) - Publish Results
(new pages/admin/PublishResults.tsx, `/admin/results/publish`):

  ShowResult=false AND has >=1 submitted attempt), "Published"

  `PUT /api/exams/{id}` (via updateExam, reusing the exam's full

  endpoint).

  Groups - if Selected Groups, only send notifications to those
  groups' members, but ShowResult itself is exam-wide since that's
  how the field actually works today, not per-group - flagged,
  not silently narrowed to imply per-student visibility control that
  doesn't exist). Publish Date & Time: "Publish Now" only (see scope
  decision). Notify Students toggle: when on, calls the EXISTING
  admin CreateNotification flow (`POST /api/notifications/admin`,
  NotificationSendToType.ExamCandidates or SelectedStudents depending
  on the Result Visibility choice) with a real "Results Published"
  notification per exam - reuses existing infra, no new notification
  type needed. Include Report Card toggle: folded into the
  notification message copy only (mentions the report card is
  downloadable) - no new gating, since generateResultPdf.ts is
  already available to any student who can see their result once
  ShowResult is true.

everything else that displays them) - kept small (2 nullable columns,
no uniqueness machinery) specifically so it doesn't balloon the way

REAL BUG CAUGHT AND FIXED BEFORE DEPLOY: ExamCode was first inserted
as a required (no-default) positional parameter in the MIDDLE of
CreateExamCommand/UpdateExamCommand/CreateExamRequest/UpdateExamRequest
(right after Title). Records with positional construction meant every
existing caller after that point would silently receive the wrong
value for the wrong field (ExamCode and Description are both
`string`, so this compiles cleanly with no error - it would have
been a silent data-corruption bug, not a build failure). Caught by
grepping for existing positional `new CreateExamCommand(...)` call
sites before deploy - found one in
Tests/ExamService/.../CreateExamHandlerTests.cs. Fixed by moving
ExamCode to the END of each record with a `= null` default,
matching the already-safe pattern used for RollNumber on the User
side from the start. Full solution rebuild + 286/286 backend tests
re-confirmed clean after the fix.

"Published (6)" / "Ready to Publish (0)" on first load - not a bug,
accurately reflecting real state. To verify the actual publish
mechanic (not just its empty state), flipped one real exam (java2)
to ShowResult=false via its real Edit Exam form, which moved it into
"Ready to Publish (1)" as expected, then published it back through
the new page's real per-row Publish button.

FEATURE (DONE, 2026-08-22): Create Notification redesign
(send_notification.png) - templates, delivery channels, Groups

=====================================================================
REQUESTED: user supplied send_notification.png, asked to update the
EXISTING Create Notification page (Notifications > Send Notification)
to match its two-column composer + delivery-summary-sidebar layout,
template dropdown, delivery-channel control, and {{variable}} message
placeholders. Confirmed 2 scope forks before planning:
- {{studentName}}/{{startDate}}/{{duration}} placeholders: build REAL
  per-recipient substitution (not literal unsubstituted text sent to
  students).
- "Delivery Channels" dropdown (In-App + Email / In-App Only / Email
  Only): build REAL channel control (not just a label over today's
  always-in-app/preference-gated-email behavior).

DATA REALITY CHECK: confirmed in
NotificationPersistenceService.CreateNotificationsAsync - title/
message are stored VERBATIM, identical for every recipient in a
batch; ScheduledAtUtc batches are "store-only" (EmailStatus stays
Pending forever, no poller ever dispatches them - a PRE-EXISTING gap,
not touched by this feature, not being silently fixed either).
{{examTitle}}/{{startDate}}/{{duration}} are BATCH-constant (same for
every recipient, depend only on the selected exam) - these get
substituted on the FRONTEND right before submit, using the exam data
CreateNotification.tsx already has via useExams(). Only
{{studentName}}/{{studentEmail}} genuinely vary per recipient inside
one batch - those need a real backend substitution pass, but since
NotificationRecipient already carries FullName/Email per recipient,
this is a plain per-entity string.Replace, no new exam-lookup client
needed.

BACKEND (NotificationService):
- Domain: EmailStatus gains `Skipped` (email intentionally not
  attempted because SendEmail was false - distinct from Pending/
  Failed so History doesn't misreport it). Notification entity gains
  `ShowInApp` bool (default true) - controls whether a row is
  surfaced to the student's in-app list; the row itself still always
  gets created (needed for admin History/delivery tracking regardless
  of channel choice, matches the mockup's "Track delivery status"
  bullet).
- Infrastructure: NotificationDbContext config + new EF migration
  (`dotnet ef migrations add`, not hand-written). NotificationRepository's
  three student-facing read paths (GetMineAsync, GetUnreadCountAsync,
  GetUnreadForUserAsync) each gain `.Where(n => n.ShowInApp)` - a
  channel-suppressed row silently never appears in the student's own
  notification list, same as it already silently respects
  ScheduledAtUtc.
- Application: CreateNotificationCommand gains `bool SendEmail = true,
  bool SendInApp = true` (defaults preserve every existing caller's
  behavior unchanged, including this session's own Publish Results
  feature which calls createNotification without specifying
  channels). INotificationPersistenceService.CreateNotificationsAsync
  gains the same two params. NotificationPersistenceService: per
  recipient, `title`/`message` get `.Replace("{{studentName}}",
  recipient.FullName).Replace("{{studentEmail}}", recipient.Email)`
  before being stored on the entity or handed to the email dispatcher;
  entity.ShowInApp = sendInApp; if sendEmail is false, EmailStatus =
  Skipped unconditionally (dispatcher never called) - otherwise
  existing isDue/preference logic unchanged.
- API: CreateNotificationHandler threads SendEmail/SendInApp from
  command to the persistence call. NotificationsController.Create
  reads request.SendEmail/SendInApp into the command.
- Shared.Contracts: CreateNotificationRequest gains `SendEmail = true,
  SendInApp = true` (defaulted, so it's additive-only for any other
  caller).

FRONTEND (CreateNotification.tsx rewritten; other notification pages
- History, Batch Details, Preferences - untouched, out of scope):
- New src/constants/notificationTemplates.ts: 5 templates, one per
  existing NotificationType value (Exam Assignment->Exam, Exam
  Reminder->Reminder, Result Published->Result, System
  Announcement->System, Account Notice->Account) - each a real
  {title, message} string containing the real {{}} placeholders
  (kept RAW/editable in the compose box after picking a template,
  matching the mockup - substitution happens at send time, not at
  template-pick time, so the admin can still hand-edit the wording).
- Layout: two-column (Col lg={8} "Notification Composer" card, Col
  lg={4} "Delivery Summary" sidebar card), matching the mockup's
  ~2:1 split.
- Notification Type: template dropdown (sets `type` + prefills Title/
  Message with the template's raw strings, only when the fields are
  still empty or match a previous template's exact text - won't
  clobber hand-edited content on a second dropdown change).
- Delivery Channels: new dropdown (In-App + Email / In-App Only /
  Email Only) driving sendEmail/sendInApp - replaces needing two
  separate toggles.
- Recipients: dropdown gains a REAL new "Groups / Batches" option
  alongside the existing All Students/Individual Students/Exam
  Candidates/Admins - resolved the same way PublishResults.tsx
  already resolves group membership (getGroup per selected group,
  dedup member ids, submitted as sendTo: 'SelectedStudents' with the
  resolved userIds - no backend change needed for this part, mirrors
  existing real infra).
- Live "N recipients selected" count next to Recipients, computed per
  target type from real data (students.length, admins.length,
  selectedUserIds.size, or summed real group memberCount for Groups).
  Exam Candidates is the one case with no cheap real client-side
  count (would need a new assignment-count lookup) - shows "Recipients
  determined at send time" instead of inventing a number.
- Options checklist: "Send email notification"/"Send in-app
  notification" become non-interactive indicator rows mirroring the
  Delivery Channels dropdown (avoids a second, redundant control for
  the same two booleans). "Include exam link" is a REAL standalone
  checkbox - when on and a related exam is selected, appends a real
  line to the message at submit time. "Track delivery status" stays
  a static informational line (already real and already true via the
  existing History/Batch Details pages - not a toggle, nothing to
  control).
- Delivery Summary sidebar: Recipients (real count/fallback text
  above), Channels (from the dropdown), Template (selected template's
  label), Status ("Ready to Send"/"Incomplete", reusing the existing
  `canSubmit` validation, now also requiring a related exam whenever
  the message still contains an exam-field placeholder), and a real
  Preview block showing Title + Message with {{examTitle}}/
  {{startDate}}/{{duration}} substituted from the selected exam (if
  any) and {{studentName}} shown as literal "Student" (a real,
  honestly-generic preview - the actual per-recipient name only
  exists server-side at send time, so previewing one specific
  student's name here would be misleading for a multi-recipient
  batch).
- Submit: substitutes {{examTitle}}/{{startDate}}/{{duration}} for
  real using the selected exam right before calling createNotification
  (matches the "batch-constant, do it client-side" reasoning above);
  {{studentName}}/{{studentEmail}} sent through untouched for the
  backend's real per-recipient pass.

`<select>` elements ONCE up front, then drove three sequential
dropdown changes off that single stale snapshot, produced a wrong
Type ("Exam" instead of "System") despite the Channel and Recipient
changes from the same script working correctly. Re-running with a
fresh `document.querySelectorAll('select')` between each dropdown
change fixed it immediately and reproduced correctly every time after
that - a script-side stale-DOM-reference artifact after React's
re-render, not a bug in the Notification Type dropdown or
`applyTemplate` itself (confirmed separately in isolation too).
