   at org-creation time with no way to ever change it again.

================================================================================
BUG FIX: CLICKING BACK INTO A COMPLETED SECTION THROWS AN ERROR
================================================================================
User report (2026-08-29): "when exam started and section first completed
then second started then i have clicked on first section then getting
error." Reproduced by reading the code path (not live, but root-caused
exactly): `TakeExam.tsx`'s live "Sections" navigator bar (during 'take'
mode, as opposed to the Review screen's own section list further down the
same file) computes whether a section button is clickable via
`canOpenSection(index)`, which only checks that EARLIER sections are
completed - it never checks whether the TARGET section itself has already
been completed and uses a non-Free (Sequential/Locked) navigation type.
So once section 1 (Sequential) is completed and section 2 becomes current,
section 1's button stays enabled - clicking it fires `switchToSection(0)`
-> `enterSection()` -> the backend correctly rejects re-entry into a
completed gated section (`EnterSectionHandler`'s own
`existingState.IsCompleted && isGated` check), surfacing as a raw
`attemptError` to the student. The Review screen's own section list
already has the correct rule for this exact case (`canReturn =
section.navigationType === 'Free' || index === sectionIndex`) - the live
Sections bar just never applied the same rule.

Also noticed while root-causing: the backend's `EnterSectionResult.
IsSectionLocked` flag (and its controller message, "This section is
locked until earlier sections are completed.") is shared between two
different situations - earlier sections not done yet, AND the target
section itself already being completed-and-gated - the message is
actively wrong/confusing for the second case. Fixing this too as
defense-in-depth even though the frontend fix should make it
unreachable through normal use.

FIX

1. `TakeExam.tsx`'s Sections navigator: block re-entry into an already-
   completed non-Free section (same rule as the Review screen's
   `canReturn`) - a completed section stays clickable only if it's Free-
   navigation or still the current section.
2. `EnterSectionResult`/`EnterSectionHandler`: split `IsSectionLocked`
   into two distinct flags so the controller can give an accurate message
   for "already completed, can't re-enter" vs. "earlier sections not done
   yet."

VERIFICATION: full solution build + backend test suite (update
EnterSectionHandler tests if any exist for the new result shape) +
frontend build + test suite, all green. Live: create a real sectioned
exam with a Sequential-navigation first section, take it as a student,
complete section 1, advance to section 2, click back on section 1 -
confirm the button is now disabled/non-clickable instead of throwing.
Sequential Docker rebuild of `submission-api` + `frontend`. Commit, push,
confirm CD deploy.

ADDENDUM (found while live-verifying the fix above): reproducing the exact
scenario live (sectioned exam, Sequential section 1, complete it, resume
into section 2) surfaced a SECOND, more severe bug in the SAME
`sectionGroupsCacheRef` mechanism added earlier this session (the shuffle-
desync fix) - `sectionsReady = !exam?.containsSections || sections !==
undefined` evaluated `true` before `exam` itself had loaded (since
`exam?.containsSections` is `undefined` while `exam` is undefined, and
`!undefined` is `true`), regardless of whether `sections` had actually
arrived. If `attemptId` resolved in that same window, the WRONG single-
group computation (every question merged into one ungrouped section) got
PERMANENTLY cached for the rest of the attempt - confirmed live via direct
API checks proving the server's per-question `sectionId` data was correct
the whole time; the corruption was 100% client-side. Fixed by requiring
`exam !== undefined` before ever deciding sections are "ready":
`sectionsReady = exam !== undefined && (!exam.containsSections || sections
!== undefined)`. Re-verified live after the fix - correct 1-question-per-
section grouping, Section 1 correctly locked/disabled after completion.

================================================================================
BUG FIX: PROGRAMMING EXAM CODE AUTO-GRADING WAS COMPLETELY BROKEN
================================================================================
User report (2026-08-29): "when programming exam created then debugger not
working expected." Investigated live, found TWO separate real bugs:

1. INFRA: `examvault-execution-worker-1` had crashed 3 days ago
   (RabbitMQ connection refused - likely a startup-order race after an
   uncontrolled Docker Desktop/host restart, since `depends_on: rabbitmq:
   condition: service_healthy` isn't re-evaluated outside `docker compose
   up`) and had NO restart policy anywhere in `docker-compose.yml` (only
   the third-party `piston` image has one) - it silently stayed dead with
   nothing auto-grading any code submission this whole time. Fixed:
   restarted it, and added `restart: unless-stopped` to it AND
   `notification-worker` (the only other RabbitMQ-consumer background
   service, same vulnerability) in docker-compose.yml.

2. AUTH (the real, deeper bug - present regardless of #1): once the
   worker WAS running and correctly picked up a `CodeAnswerSubmitted`
   event, ran the code via Execution Service, and tried to PUT the grade
   back to Submission Service's `/api/submissions/{attemptId}/answers/
   {questionId}/grade` endpoint - that call was rejected with 403.
   Root cause: the endpoint requires BOTH `[Authorize(Roles = "Admin")]`
   AND `[Authorize(Policy = Exams)]` (a per-tenant plan-feature gate).
   `SystemTokenProvider` (the worker's own service-to-service JWT minter)
   set role="Admin" but included NO feature claims at all - only
   "SuperAdmin" bypasses feature policies, so this token failed the
   Exams feature check on every single call, unconditionally, regardless
   of the calling tenant's real plan. This means code-question
   auto-grading has likely never worked correctly since this pattern was
   built - not a recent regression. Fixed: added a `Feature:Exams` claim
   to the minted token.

VERIFICATION: interactive "Run Code" (test-case checking during the
exam) was independently confirmed working correctly the whole time (a
separate, synchronous execution-api call unaffected by either bug) -
live browser test showed 2/2 test cases passing with real Piston
execution. The auto-grading fix was verified with a real, live,
end-to-end run: submitted a real Programming exam answer twice (once
pre-fix, confirming the 403; once post-fix, rebuilt execution-worker
image, bumped the assignment's maxAttempts to allow a retry) - the
second submission's grade PUT returned 200 (not 403), and the student's
own `GET /api/submissions/mine` confirmed `marksAwarded: 10`,
`gradedByUserId` set to the system sentinel id, `gradedAtUtc` populated.
Full solution build green (no existing unit tests cover the Worker
project directly - this is infra/service-to-service auth, verified via
live integration testing instead). Sequential Docker rebuild of
execution-worker done as part of verification. Test exam archived and
deleted afterward.

PLAN: EXAM TYPE WISE REPORT DRILL-DOWN PAGES (2026-09-01)
================================================================================
User supplied `Exam-type-subscreen.png`, a 6-screen wireframe for the
existing "Exam Type Wise Report" (added earlier today, commit c4690ce) -
that page was screen 1 (Overview) only, with its row Action icon dead-
linking to the generic Exam Reports list. This plan adds the other 5
screens as real drill-down pages, all client-side aggregation over
already-loaded data (useExams/useExamTypes/useAdminResultsForAllExams/
useAttemptsByExam) - no backend changes, same pattern as c4690ce.

New shared hook `hooks/useExamTypeReportData.ts`: given a typeId, returns
{ examType, examsOfType, resultsOfType, attemptsOfType, isLoading } -
factors out the "filter everything down to one exam type" join that all
4 new type-scoped pages need, avoiding 4x duplication.

New pages (all `active="Exam Type Wise Report"` in AdminLayout, all
Admin-only routes under /admin/reports/...):
1. ExamTypeDetails.tsx (/exam-type/:typeId) - type header (icon/name/
   purpose), 4 stat cards, search+status+export, paginated table of the
   type's exams (row action -> existing ExamReportDetails per-exam page,
   reused as-is), plus 3 link-buttons to the pages below.
2. ExamTypePerformanceAnalysis.tsx (/exam-type/:typeId/performance) - 5
   stat cards, tab strip (Score Distribution/Pass vs Fail/Average Score
   Trend/Completion Rate/Top Performing Exams). Completion Rate reuses
   useAttemptsByExam's status!=='InProgress' pattern already established
   in AdminReports.tsx.
3. ExamTypeStudentPerformance.tsx (/exam-type/:typeId/students) - same
   per-student aggregation as StudentReports.tsx, scoped to this type's
   exams only.
4. ExamTypeComparison.tsx (/exam-type-wise/comparison, reached from the
   Overview page's new header button, not type-scoped) - tab strip
   (Average Score/Pass %/Participants/Completion Rate/Attempts) each
   rendered as a bar chart across all exam types via a new shared
   `useExamTypeSummaryRows()` hook extracted from ExamTypeWiseReport.tsx's
   existing `rows` useMemo (so Overview and Comparison compute identical
   numbers), plus a dynamic "highest/lowest" insight line.
5. ExamTypeQuestionAnalysis.tsx (/exam-type/:typeId/questions) - 5 stat
   cards, tab strip (Overall Analysis/Most Difficult/Most Skipped),
   reuses ExamReportDetails.tsx's per-question correct/incorrect/
   unanswered aggregation generalized across every exam of the type.

New small shared component `components/reports/ReportTabs.tsx` (a plain
underline tab strip) - no tab pattern existed yet in Reports.

Two wireframe fields are NOT implemented as fabricated data, per this
project's no-dead-UI convention: Question Analysis's "Average Difficulty"
and "Avg Time per Question" have no backing data anywhere in this
codebase (questions carry no numeric difficulty score, no per-question
timing is tracked) - substituted with two numbers that ARE real
("Total Attempts Analyzed", "Questions with 100% Correct"). Most
Difficult/Skipped Questions tables drop the wireframe's per-row eye-icon
Action column - no per-question detail page exists in this app and
adding one is out of scope for this ask.

Existing ExamTypeWiseReport.tsx (Overview) changes: row Action ->
`/admin/reports/exam-type/{id}` instead of `/admin/reports/exams`; new
"Compare Exam Types" button next to Export Report -> Comparison page.

PLAN: SPLIT LiveMonitoring INTO ExamSecurity / LiveMonitoring / Proctoring (2026-09-04)
================================================================================
Trigger: shubham-admin@examvaults.in kept seeing/using the whole "Live
Monitoring" admin console section after Live Monitoring was revoked on
their org's Plan - traced to the single `LiveMonitoring` PlanFeature
bundling four unrelated capabilities (Active Exams, Student Attempts,
Security Violations, camera-based Proctoring) behind one on/off switch.
User proposed a 3-tier entitlement redesign instead of a 2-tier one:
ExamSecurity (non-video browser/security controls + violation tracking,
should be available even on lower plans), LiveMonitoring narrowed to
real-time non-video oversight (active exams/attempts/progress/violation
feed/admin intervention), and Proctoring kept as the premium,
privacy-sensitive webcam/live-video/recording tier. Explicitly NOT adding
AI Proctoring or Identity Verification - neither exists in this codebase
today, per the user's own instruction not to invent capabilities.

Inspected first (before writing this plan), per the user's own
instruction: `PlanFeature` (Backend/Shared/.../Multitenancy/PlanFeature.cs)
is a shared enum; `Plan.IncludedFeatures` is stored as a comma-separated
list of enum NAMES (nvarchar(200)) via a ValueConverter in
UserDbContext.cs, not a bitmask - so appending new enum values is safe
regardless of position, no int-renumbering risk. Every service
(Submission/Exam/User) auto-registers a `Feature:{Name}` AuthorizationOptions
policy for every enum value via `Enum.GetValues<PlanFeature>()` reflection
in each service's own `FeaturePolicies.AddFeaturePolicies()` - adding a
new enum value alone creates its policy everywhere already; only need to
add named consts where a controller actually references one via
`[Authorize(Policy = ...)]` (which requires a compile-time constant).
Grepped the ENTIRE backend for `Policy = LiveMonitoring` and confirmed
all 6 uses live in exactly one file, `SubmissionsController.cs` - no
other service references this feature. `ProctoringSettingsController`
(Exam Service, the per-exam detector-config screen matching `7.png`) is
already gated by `Settings`, not `LiveMonitoring` - confirmed unrelated,
staying untouched (see scope note below). The seeded "Full Access" plan
(`TenantConstants.FullAccessPlanId`) auto-includes every enum value via
`Enum.GetValues<PlanFeature>().ToList()` in its `HasData` seed - EF's
migration generator re-diffs this automatically when the enum grows, so
that one row updates itself via the new migration with no manual SQL.
Any OTHER, manually-created Plan does NOT get this treatment automatically
(HasData only ever touches its own seeded rows) - needs an explicit
backward-compat data migration step, see step 3.

Concrete steps:

1. Backend, shared enum: add `ExamSecurity` and `Proctoring` to
   `PlanFeature` (Backend/Shared/OnlineExamSystem.Shared.Common/
   Multitenancy/PlanFeature.cs), appended at the end - safe since storage
   is by name, not numeric position.

2. Backend, re-tag `SubmissionsController.cs` (Submission.API):
   - `LiveByExam` (GET by-exam/{id}/live, powers Active Exams) and
     `ForceSubmit` (admin ends an attempt) stay `LiveMonitoring`.
   - `ViolationsByExam` (GET by-exam/{id}/violations) and
     `UpdateViolationStatus` (PUT violations/{id}/status) move to the new
     `ExamSecurity` policy.
   - `SetLiveWatch` (PUT {id}/live-watch) and `WatchRecording`
     (POST {id}/recording/watch) move to the new `Proctoring` policy.
   - `JoinRecording` (POST {id}/recording/join, the student's own camera
     publish) currently has NO feature gate at all - add `Proctoring`.
     This is a genuinely new enforcement point, not a re-tag: closes a
     real gap where a student could still publish video for an org with
     no Proctoring entitlement at all. Flagged explicitly since it's new
     behavior, not just a rename.
   Add `ExamSecurity`/`Proctoring` consts to Submission Service's own
   `Authorization/FeaturePolicies.cs` (same file `LiveMonitoring`'s const
   already lives in).

3. Backend, EF migration (`dotnet ef migrations add
   SplitLiveMonitoringFeature --project ...User.Infrastructure`):
   auto-picks-up the Full-Access plan's HasData string change as an
   UpdateData call. Add a hand-written `migrationBuilder.Sql(...)` in the
   same migration's Up(): for every OTHER Plan row whose IncludedFeatures
   currently contains "LiveMonitoring", append ",ExamSecurity,Proctoring"
   (idempotent - only if not already present) so no organization already
   granted Live Monitoring silently loses Security Violations or
   Proctoring access it effectively had yesterday. This is the
   backward-compatibility guarantee the user explicitly asked for.
   Migration gets applied and verified against a real local DB copy
   before this is considered done - never assumed blind against the live
   Hostinger VPS database.

4. Frontend, `types/plan.ts`: add `ExamSecurity`/`Proctoring` to the
   `PlanFeature` union, `ALL_PLAN_FEATURES`, and `PLAN_FEATURE_LABELS`
   ("Exam Security", "Proctoring").

5. Frontend, `pages/platform/SubscriptionPlans.tsx`: new `MODULE_INFO`
   entries (icon/color/description) for both new modules. Regroup the
   Create/Edit modal's "Included Modules" card grid into labeled
   sections matching the user's own proposed layout - "Exam Security" as
   its own section, "Monitoring & Proctoring" grouping Live Monitoring +
   Proctoring together - rather than the current flat 2-column grid, so
   the plan-editing screen itself visually teaches the same 3-tier model
   (why this problem won't recur when a 4th tier like AI Proctoring gets
   added later).

6. Frontend, `components/AdminSidebar.tsx`: give `NavChild` its own
   optional `feature` field (currently only `NavItem`/top-level groups
   have one, added in the prior LiveMonitoring-only fix). Live Monitoring
   group's children get individual features: Active Exams/Student
   Attempts -> `LiveMonitoring`, Security Violations -> `ExamSecurity`,
   Proctoring -> `Proctoring`. The parent group itself is visible if ANY
   child passes (not one blanket feature check on the group) - an org
   with only ExamSecurity should still see "Live Monitoring" in the nav
   with just Security Violations under it, not have the whole section
   vanish.

7. Frontend, `routes/AppRoutes.tsx`: update the 4 live-monitoring routes'
   `ProtectedRoute feature=` prop to match step 2's table - Security
   Violations -> `ExamSecurity`, Proctoring -> `Proctoring`, Active
   Exams/Student Attempts stay `LiveMonitoring`.

8. Verification: this codebase's own established convention for closing
   every prior phase/feature gate is live curl/browser adversarial
   testing with a real JWT (see every "Verified live..." paragraph
   throughout this file) - there is no WebApplicationFactory-style
   integration-test project anywhere for controller-level authorization,
   so this plan follows the same convention rather than introducing new
   test infrastructure unprompted. Concretely: create a throwaway Plan
   with only ExamSecurity checked and confirm Security Violations works
   while Active Exams/Student Attempts/Proctoring 403; repeat with only
   LiveMonitoring checked and only Proctoring checked; confirm the
   sidebar shows exactly the right subset of Live Monitoring's children
   in each case; confirm the backward-compat migration left an existing
   LiveMonitoring-only Plan with all three now checked.

Explicitly OUT of scope, decided during planning rather than silently
dropped: `ProctoringSettingsController` (the per-exam detector-config
screen, `7.png` - Enable Proctoring/Face Detection/Allow Live Video Feed
toggles) stays gated by `Settings`, untouched - configuring detectors is
an Admin Settings capability, distinct from the runtime entitlement to
actually USE Live Monitoring/Proctoring, and touching it would mean
splitting one settings-update endpoint's authorization per-field, which
wasn't asked for. "Candidate activity logs" as its own screen doesn't
exist anywhere in this codebase and is not being built now. No AI
Proctoring or Identity Verification PlanFeature - flagged in the user's
own message as future-only, added if/when the backend actually supports
them.

DONE (2026-09-04): all 8 steps implemented exactly as planned, no
deviations. `PlanFeature` gained `ExamSecurity = 8`/`Proctoring = 9`.
`SubmissionsController.cs` re-tagged: `ViolationsByExam`/
`UpdateViolationStatus` -> `ExamSecurity`; `SetLiveWatch`/`WatchRecording`
-> `Proctoring`; `LiveByExam`/`ForceSubmit` stayed `LiveMonitoring`;
`JoinRecording` gained `[Authorize(Policy = Proctoring)]` (previously
ungated entirely - the one genuinely new enforcement point, not a
re-tag). EF migration `SplitLiveMonitoringFeature` generated against
`OnlineExamSystem.User.Infrastructure` - auto-scaffolded UpdateData for
the seeded Full-Access plan, hand-added the backward-compat raw-SQL
UPDATE (comma-wrapped LIKE, idempotent) for every other Plan row that
had LiveMonitoring, plus a best-effort symmetric Down(). Frontend:
`types/plan.ts` grew to 10 features; `SubscriptionPlans.tsx`'s Create/
Edit modal now renders 3 labeled groups (general modules unchanged,
"Exam Security" section, "Monitoring & Proctoring" section with Live
Monitoring + Proctoring together) via a new shared `renderModuleCard`
helper, with new `ShieldCheckIcon`/`CameraIcon`; `AdminSidebar.tsx`'s
`NavChild` gained its own optional `feature` (previously only `NavItem`
had one) - Live Monitoring's 4 children now gate individually
(Active Exams/Student Attempts -> LiveMonitoring, Security Violations ->
ExamSecurity, Proctoring -> Proctoring), and the parent group is visible
whenever ANY child is; `AppRoutes.tsx`'s Security Violations/Proctoring
routes' `ProtectedRoute feature=` updated to match.

Verification done: `dotnet build` clean across the whole solution,
`dotnet test` 369/369 across all 8 backend test projects (zero
regressions - none of these are new test coverage, since this codebase's
existing authorization-policy verification convention is live curl/
browser testing, not unit tests, per step 8's own note). Frontend
`tsc --noEmit`, `oxlint`, and a real `npm run build` (production build,
not just tsc) all clean.

NOT done, explicitly left for the user rather than assumed: the EF
migration was generated and reviewed but NOT applied to any database.
`dotnet ef migrations list` against this machine's local
`ExamVault.UserDb` showed 19 OTHER migrations already Pending before this
one even existed - applying `dotnet ef database update` would have
silently applied that entire unrelated backlog as a side effect of this
task, on a database whose current real state wasn't confirmed, so it was
deliberately not run. The user needs to apply this migration themselves
(locally, and separately on the Hostinger VPS's production database once

existing Plans' access before rollout. Step 8's live curl/browser
adversarial verification (throwaway Plan with only ExamSecurity/only

combination) was also not performed - requires a real logged-in Super
Admin session this agent cannot create itself (entering passwords is
off-limits), same constraint noted earlier in this session's Live
Monitoring fix.

PLAN: REMOVE GENERAL SETTINGS AND SYSTEM SETTINGS FROM ADMIN CONSOLE (2026-09-04)
================================================================================
User decided the Admin console's "General Settings" and "System Settings"
cards (Admin > Settings) shouldn't exist for tenant Admins at all - full
removal (entity/backend/frontend/data), not just hiding the UI. Confirmed
with the user before touching anything, since this is a real, irreversible
schema change (DROP TABLE via migration).

Inspected both features fully before removing anything:
- General Settings (Exam Service): `GeneralSettings` entity (Organization
  Name/Support Email/Language/Timezone/Date Format), one row per tenant.
  Fully self-contained - only its own Get/UpdateGeneralSettingsHandler
  ever reads or writes it, confirmed via a repo-wide grep. Safe to remove
  cleanly, no other functional loss.
- System Settings (Notification Service): `SystemSettings` entity
  (MaintenanceModeEnabled/BackupFrequency/AuditLogRetentionDays/LogLevel).
  MaintenanceModeEnabled/LogLevel/BackupFrequency really are inert
  (persisted, never wired to real behavior - the entity's own doc comment
  says so). AuditLogRetentionDays is NOT inert - `AuditLogRetentionCleanup
  Service` (a real 24h-polling BackgroundService) reads it per-tenant to
  delete old AuditLog rows. Flagged this to the user before proceeding;
  user chose to remove the cleanup job too rather than keep it on a
  hardcoded default - audit logs will grow unbounded with no automatic
  cleanup from this point on, a deliberate, explicit tradeoff.

Full removal list (confirmed via repo-wide grep, no other consumers):
- Frontend: `GeneralSettingsPage.tsx`, `SystemSettingsPage.tsx`,
  `useSystemSettings.ts`, `systemSettingsApi.ts`, `types/systemSettings.ts`,
  `useGeneralSettings`/`getGeneralSettings`/`updateGeneralSettings` out of
  `useExams.ts`/`examApi.ts`, `GeneralSettingsResponse` out of
  `types/exam.ts`, the two routes in `AppRoutes.tsx`, and the 'general'/
  'system' cards + their row-building code out of `AdminSettings.tsx`.
- Backend (Exam Service): `GeneralSettingsController.cs`,
  `GeneralSettings.cs` entity, `Settings/GetGeneralSettings/` and
  `Settings/UpdateGeneralSettings/` folders, the GeneralSettings members
  on `IExamRepository`/`ExamRepository`, its `ExamDbContext` DbSet/config,
  Program.cs DI, `Shared.Contracts` request/response, `FakeExamRepository`
  in the test project. New migration drops the GeneralSettings table.
- Backend (Notification Service): `SystemSettingsController.cs`,
  `AuditLogRetentionCleanupService.cs` (the job, per the user's choice),
  `SystemSettings.cs` entity, `ISystemSettingsRepository`/
  `SystemSettingsRepository`, `Settings/GetSystemSettings/` and
  `Settings/UpdateSystemSettings/` folders, `NotificationDbContext`
  DbSet/config, Program.cs DI (repository + handlers + hosted service),
  `Shared.Contracts` request/response. New migration drops the
  SystemSettings table.

Verification: full solution `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` (confirm no
regressions in the other 350+ tests - none directly tested either
handler), frontend `tsc --noEmit`/lint/`npm run build`. Migrations
generated and reviewed but, per this session's own established caution
around this local machine's database (19 unrelated migrations already
sat pending before this session started), NOT applied automatically -
left for the user to apply deliberately, same as the LiveMonitoring

================================================================================
FIX: WIRE EXAM SETTINGS' DEFAULTS INTO NEWLY CREATED EXAMS (2026-09-04)
================================================================================
User reported (Admin side): configured "Exam Settings" defaults weren't
showing up on newly created exams. Confirmed via the entity's own doc
comment on `ExamDefaults.cs` - literally said "Not yet wired into
CreateExam's actual prefill values (deferred)". Every new `ExamPaper`
always got the entity's hardcoded C# property-initializer defaults
(MaxAttempts=1, NegativeMarkingEnabled=false, NegativeMarks=0,
AutoSubmitOnTimeEnd=true) regardless of what the Admin configured.

Checked which of ExamDefaults' 9 fields have a real corresponding field
to seed before wiring anything: `DefaultDurationMinutes`/
`PassingScorePercent` map to `CreateExamRequest.durationMinutes`/
`passingMarks`, both collected directly on the Create Exam form itself -
fixed client-side (`CreateExam.tsx` now prefills via `useExamDefaults()`,
guarded so it only applies while those two fields still match the
untouched hardcoded initial state, never clobbering something the Admin
already typed). `DefaultMaxAttempts`/`NegativeMarkingEnabled`/
`NegativeMarkingValue`/`AutoSubmitEnabled` map to real `ExamPaper` fields
never collected on the Create form at all - fixed server-side
(`CreateExamHandler` now calls the already-existing
`GetOrCreateExamDefaultsAsync` and seeds the new exam from it instead of
the hardcoded initializers). `AutoSaveEnabled`/`QuestionNavigationMode`/
`ResultPublishingMode` have no corresponding `ExamPaper` field anywhere -
confirmed via the entity's full field list, left deferred rather than
inventing new columns for this ask. Updated both entities' own doc
comments and `ExamSettingsPage.tsx`'s stale "don't yet prefill" note to
match.

Verified live end-to-end (throwaway Admin account, same pattern as
earlier fixes today): set distinctive test ExamDefaults (MaxAttempts=7,
NegativeMarkingEnabled=false, NegativeMarks=0.5, AutoSubmitEnabled=false,
all different from the old hardcoded values), created a real exam via
curl, confirmed the response carried exactly those values - not the old
1/false/0/true. Restored the tenant's original ExamDefaults and deleted
the throwaway exam/account afterward. `dotnet test` for Exam Service
still 83/83 (no test asserted on the old hardcoded values). Frontend
prefill (Duration/Passing Marks) is client-side-only logic with no
backend round-trip to verify via curl - confirmed by code review and
`tsc`/lint/`npm run build`, not live-clicked (needs a real browser
session this agent can't create itself).

PLAN: PLAN PRICING & PER-PLAN USAGE LIMITS (2026-09-04)
================================================================================
User supplied `create-plan.png`, a richer Create/Edit Plan modal than
today's (Plan Information + Included Modules only) - adds Pricing &
Billing, Usage Limits, Trial Settings, and a separate Advanced Features
checklist. Asked for a plan first, scoped down via a clarifying question
before writing this: **Trial Settings and Advanced Features are OUT of
scope** (neither was picked when asked) - this plan covers only Pricing
& Billing and Usage Limits. Also confirmed: Usage Limits enforcement
extends what already exists rather than building everything the mockup
implies (Questions/AI Questions per Month/Storage GB stay honestly
unenforced, no new infrastructure for those); Pricing is a stored+
displayed reference value only, not connected to any billing/payment
system (none exists in this codebase).

Inspected the current real state before designing this:
- `Plan` (User Service): Name/Description/IncludedFeatures only - no
  pricing, no limits. Its own doc comment previously said "Billing/
  pricing stays out of scope" - this plan deliberately reopens that,
  per the user's explicit ask this time.
- `Tenant` already has `MaxUsers`/`MaxExams`/`MaxStudents` (nullable =
  unlimited). MaxUsers is genuinely enforced (CreateUserHandler, flat
  total across every role). MaxExams is genuinely enforced
  (ExamService's CreateExamHandler, via a real cross-service call -
  `ITenantLimitsClient`/`GetTenantLimitsHandler`). MaxStudents is
  stored but its own doc comment admits it - "not enforced yet - no
  per-role count check anywhere today, only a flat per-tenant user
  count." No MaxAdmins/MaxInstructors field exists at all.
- These Tenant limits are seeded ONCE, at tenant creation
  (`CreateTenantHandler`), from `PlatformSettings.DefaultMaxUsers/
  DefaultMaxExams/DefaultMaxStudents` (one global Super-Admin-set
  default for every tenant regardless of Plan) - then individually
  overridable per org thereafter. Nothing currently ties these limits
  to which Plan a tenant is on, which somewhat defeats having
  differentiated pricing tiers - this plan's real point is closing
  that gap, not just adding display fields.
- `AssignPlanToTenantHandler` (the "Change Plan / Upgrade" action
  already used live today on Shubham Institute) currently only flips
  `Tenant.PlanId` - doesn't touch limits at all.

Concrete steps:

1. Backend, `Plan.cs`: add `MonthlyPrice`/`AnnualPrice` (decimal?) and
   `MaxStudents`/`MaxAdmins`/`MaxInstructors`/`MaxExams`/`MaxQuestions`/
   `MaxAiQuestionsPerMonth`/`StorageGb` (int?, nullable = unlimited,
   same convention Tenant already uses). Thread through
   CreatePlanCommand/UpdatePlanCommand/Handlers/Validators,
   PlansController, Shared.Contracts request/response, `types/plan.ts`.

2. Backend, `Tenant.cs`: add `MaxAdmins`/`MaxInstructors` (int?),
   parallel to the existing `MaxStudents`.

3. Backend, `CreateTenantHandler`: seed the new tenant's
   MaxUsers/MaxAdmins/MaxInstructors/MaxStudents/MaxExams from the
   ASSIGNED PLAN's limits instead of `PlatformSettings.DefaultMax*`.
   Flagged, not silently done: this makes `PlatformSettings.
   DefaultMaxUsers/DefaultMaxExams/DefaultMaxStudents` (Platform
   Settings' own "Tenant Defaults" section) effectively dead - not
   deleting that page/entity in this pass (a separate decision, same
   as the General/System Settings removal earlier today needed its
   own explicit go-ahead), just noting it as a real follow-up.

4. Backend, `AssignPlanToTenantHandler`: when the plan actually
   changes (not a no-op reassignment to the same plan), also re-seed
   the tenant's MaxUsers/MaxAdmins/MaxInstructors/MaxStudents/MaxExams
   from the NEW plan's limits - real "upgrade your quota" semantics,
   matching what a plan change should actually do. This is a genuine
   behavior change to an action already used live today, called out
   explicitly rather than silently bundled in.

5. Backend, real enforcement extension (`CreateUserHandler`): add a
   new `IUserRepository.CountByTenantAndRoleAsync(tenantId, role)`,
   then check the new user's specific role against
   Tenant.MaxStudents/MaxAdmins/MaxInstructors (Student/Admin/
   Instructor respectively) alongside the existing flat MaxUsers
   check, which stays as an overall safety net, unchanged. This is
   what actually turns MaxStudents from stored-but-inert into real,
   and adds real Admin/Instructor limits that never existed before.

6. Backend, EF migration: new nullable columns on `Plans`
   (MonthlyPrice/AnnualPrice/MaxStudents/MaxAdmins/MaxInstructors/
   MaxExams/MaxQuestions/MaxAiQuestionsPerMonth/StorageGb) and
   `Tenants` (MaxAdmins/MaxInstructors). Generated and reviewed but
   NOT applied automatically, same caution as every migration this
   session - this local machine's DB already had unrelated pending
   migrations before this session started.

7. Frontend, `SubscriptionPlans.tsx`: add "Pricing & Billing" (Monthly
   Price/Annual Price, ₹ prefix, an "Annual billing saves ~17%"
   helper note computed live from the two entered values, matching
   the mockup's own math rather than a hardcoded "17%") and "Usage
   Limits" (Students/Admins/Instructors/Exams/Questions/AI Questions
   per Month/Storage GB - blank input = unlimited, matching Tenant's
   own existing null-means-unlimited UI convention) sections to the
   Create/Edit modal, using the same icon-badge SectionBar pattern
   built for Exam Settings today. Questions/AI Questions per Month/
   Storage GB fields get a small honest note that they're not yet
   enforced, matching this codebase's own established disclosure
   convention rather than presenting them as identically real to the
   four that are.

8. Frontend, `OrganizationsAndPlans.tsx`/`OrganizationDetails.tsx`:
   NOT changed in this pass - flagged as a reasonable follow-up
   (showing a tenant's real current limits/price next to its plan)
   rather than silently expanding scope beyond the Create Plan modal
   itself, which is what was actually asked for.

9. Verification: `dotnet build`/`test` (full solution, watch for any
   test building a "valid" CreateUserCommand/Plan that this changes
   the meaning of, same class of gap the Exam Type change hit this
   session), frontend `tsc`/lint/`npm run build`/`npm run test` (all
   four this time, not just three - the Exam Type CI failure earlier
   today was exactly this). Live curl verification with the
   established throwaway-account pattern: create a test Plan with
   tight Student/Admin/Instructor/Exam limits, assign it to a
   throwaway tenant, confirm user/exam creation is genuinely blocked
   once each limit is hit and allowed below it - not just that the
   fields save and redisplay correctly.

NOT started yet - plan only, per the user's explicit request to review
this before any code changes.

this one route's Roles to "Admin,Instructor", matching what

build+test still 390/390 passing afterward.

DONE (2026-09-05): Fixed a real dark-mode bug reported in My Profile >
Preferences - selecting Dark theme only partially applied: individual
react-bootstrap components (Cards, the profile info panel, notification
panel) correctly restyled via Bootstrap 5.3's data-bs-theme CSS variables,
but the page's own main-content wrapper and top bar stayed stuck light,
leaving a jarring half-dark/half-light result exactly as shown in the
reported screenshot.

Root cause: AdminLayout/InstructorLayout/PlatformLayout/StudentLayout (all
4 - the shared shell every single dashboard page renders inside) used
Bootstrap's bg-light/bg-white utility classes for the main content area
and top bar. Those two classes are deliberately STATIC in Bootstrap 5.3's
color-mode system - they never respond to data-bs-theme, unlike the
adaptive bg-body/bg-body-tertiary classes. Fixed all 4 layouts:
bg-light -> bg-body-tertiary (main content), bg-white -> bg-body (top
bar) - matches Bootstrap's own documented dark-mode convention.

Also confirmed, not a bug: the user asked whether "System" and "Dark"
looked the same - they do on a machine whose OS/browser is itself set to
a dark color-scheme preference (confirmed via
matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)').matches === true on the test
browser), since System deliberately mirrors that OS preference. They'd
only visibly differ on a light-OS machine.

Verification: tsc clean, lint clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings), build
succeeded, 61/61 Vitest tests passed. Live-verified against the QA Demo

DONE (2026-09-05): Fixed a third real dark-mode bug - hovering over a
table row (e.g. the Organizations list) made its text nearly unreadable.
Root cause: index.css's `.table-hover > tbody > tr:hover > *` rule
hardcodes a light hover background (#f8f9ff) with no dark-mode
counterpart - in dark mode the row's own text goes light (correctly,
via Bootstrap's theme-aware body color) while this override kept forcing
a light background regardless of theme, producing light-text-on-light-
background on hover. Added a `[data-bs-theme='dark']` override with a
proper dark, subtle hover tint (rgba(255,255,255,0.075)), leaving the
existing light-mode color untouched. Checked the rest of index.css for
the same class of bug - found a couple of hardcoded light borders
(.dropdown-menu, .form-control/.form-select) that stay light in dark
mode too, but those are minor border-visibility cosmetics, not the
text-becomes-invisible severity that was actually reported, so left
alone rather than scope-creeping.

Verification: tsc clean, lint clean, build succeeded, 61/61 tests passed.
Rebuilt the Docker frontend and live-verified by hovering the exact same
row from the bug report (Test Org, Inactive) - now shows a proper dark
hover highlight with fully legible text.

DONE (2026-09-05): Security audit - "who created/changed this record" (actor
accountability) across every real DB table in every service. BaseEntity only
gives Id + CreatedAtUtc, no CreatedBy at all; that must be added per-entity.
Grepped every `DbSet<>` across all 5 services' DbContexts (~34 real tables)
and classified each one. Already correct (no gap): ExamPaper/ExamQuestion
(CreatedByUserId), Notification (CreatedByAdminUserId), AuditLog (UserId IS
the actor), ViolationEvent/SystemErrorLog (resolver tracking), every
self-owned row via its own UserId, every child/detail table that inherits
accountability from its parent, and system-generated logs with no human
creator concept. Result/AI/Execution services have no DbSets of their own
(stateless/aggregator) - nothing to check there.

Found 7 real gaps and fixed all 7 (user confirmed "yes" to full scope, not
just High severity):
  High (security-critical config, zero actor tracking before this):
  - PlatformSettings (User Service) -> UpdatedByUserId
  - RolePermission (User Service) -> UpdatedByUserId
  - ProctoringSettings (Exam Service) -> UpdatedByUserId
  Medium (real records missing creator attribution):
  - Tenant (User Service) -> CreatedByUserId
  - Plan (User Service) -> CreatedByUserId + UpdatedByUserId
  - AppUser/Users (User Service) -> CreatedByUserId (null for
    self-registered accounts - there is no admin creator for those)
  - ExamAssignment (Exam Service) -> CreatedByUserId (the Admin who
    scheduled the sitting - controls proctoring/live-video on
    potentially many students' attempts)
Left alone as Low/cosmetic (out of scope): Group, ExamType,
ReminderSettings, ExamDefaults, NotificationTemplate.

For each of the 7: added the DB column, threaded the real caller's id from
the controller's own JWT claim (ClaimTypes.NameIdentifier - the same
pattern ExamPaper/ExamQuestion already used) through Command -> Handler ->
entity, and surfaced it on the relevant Response DTO. RolePermission was the
most involved - its diff-based ReplaceForRoleAsync repository method stamps
UpdatedByUserId on both newly-added AND surviving rows on every save, and is
called from two separate paths (SuperAdmin's cross-tenant edit via
TenantsController, and a tenant Admin's own self-service edit via
RolesController) that both needed the same treatment; GetAllRolePermissionsHandler's
per-role aggregation was updated to report whichever actor touched the
rows at the role's own max UpdatedAtUtc. AppUser has two real creation
paths - CreateUserHandler (Admin adds a user to their own tenant) and
CreateTenantAdminHandler (SuperAdmin bootstraps a brand-new tenant's first
Admin) - both wired.

Scope was deliberately backend-only (the data now genuinely exists and is
captured); no new UI screens were added to display these fields - that's a
reasonable separate follow-up if wanted, not implied by the original
security/audit ask.

Caught and fixed 3 real fake/test breakages caused by adding non-defaulted
new parameters (CreatedAssignmentCommand, UpdateRolePermissionsCommand,
IRolePermissionRepository.ReplaceForRoleAsync all gained a required Guid,
which is correct - an actor should never silently default to empty on
these particular calls): FakeRolePermissionRepository's interface
implementation, and 4 positional test-call sites across
CreateAssignmentHandlerTests.cs and UpdateRolePermissionsValidatorTests.cs.

Verification: dotnet build clean on both User Service and Exam Service (0
warnings, 0 errors). Full test suites green: User Service Application.Tests
94/94, Exam Service Application.Tests 84/84. Generated (not applied) one EF
Core migration per service - User Service's AddCreatedByAuditFields (Users,
Tenants, RolePermissions, PlatformSettings, Plans) and Exam Service's
AddCreatedByAuditFields (ProctoringSettings, ExamAssignments) - reviewed
both by hand; ExamAssignment.CreatedByUserId is correctly non-nullable with
existing rows backfilled to Guid.Empty (that column didn't exist before),
every other new column is nullable since it only applies going forward.
Per this session's standing convention, did NOT run `dotnet ef database
update` - migrations apply automatically on the next local container
rebuild/restart.

NOT committed/pushed - holding per the user's standing rule.

PLAN (2026-09-05): Follow-up to the CreatedBy audit above - user asked to
actually SHOW the Created By field everywhere Super Admin is looking, not
just have it captured in the DB. Scoping: of the 7 tables from the audit,
only 5 have any Super Admin-facing screen at all - ProctoringSettings
(ProctoringSettingsPage.tsx) and ExamAssignment (AssignExam.tsx) are both
Admin-only pages with no Super Admin route, so they're excluded here.

The 5 in scope and where they render for Super Admin:
  - Tenant/Organizations -> ManageTenants.tsx (list) + OrganizationDetails.tsx
    (detail, Overview tab) - both already show "Created On" but not who.
  - Plan -> SubscriptionPlans.tsx (card grid) - shows no metadata at all
    today, not even the existing CreatedAtUtc/UpdatedAtUtc.
  - AppUser/Users -> AllUsers.tsx (list) - shows "Joined On" but not who
    added the account.
  - PlatformSettings -> PlatformSettings.tsx (single record) - shows no
    "last updated" metadata at all today.
  - RolePermission -> OrganizationDetails.tsx (Settings tab, Super Admin's
    per-tenant Role Permissions editor) - currently returns NO metadata at
    all (TenantsController's two GET call sites literally hardcode
    `new RolePermissionsResponse(role, permissions, null)` since
    GetTenantRolePermissionsHandler only ever fetched permission keys).

Approach: resolve the stored actor Guid into a display name server-side
and embed it as a new `CreatedByName`/`UpdatedByName` string right on the
existing Response DTOs - this already matches the one real precedent in
the codebase, NotificationService's AuditLogResponse.UserName (the
frontend there just renders `log.userName ?? '-'` with zero client-side
lookup logic). A small reusable ActorNameResolver helper (User.API) wraps
the already-existing IUserRepository.GetByIdsAsync batch method - no new
backend endpoint needed, and no new DB column either (this is a read-time
join, not a stored value), so no new migration.

RolePermission needs one extra piece: since GetTenantRolePermissionsHandler
never fetched metadata at all, adding a new IRolePermissionRepository
method (GetForRoleWithMetadataAsync - deliberately NOT touching the
existing GetForRoleAsync, which is on the hot path for every login/token-
refresh) that returns permissions + UpdatedAtUtc + UpdatedByUserId,
mirroring the aggregation GetAllRolePermissionsHandler already does
(max UpdatedAtUtc across the role's rows, whichever row(s) share that max
give the actor).

Frontend: add the new fields to types/tenant.ts, types/plan.ts,
types/user.ts (PlatformUserListItem), types/platformSettings.ts, and a
named type for the tenant role-permissions response (replacing the
inline type in tenantsApi.ts that currently discards everything except
`.permissions`). New UI: a "Created By" column on ManageTenants.tsx and
AllUsers.tsx; a "Created By" line on OrganizationDetails.tsx's Overview
tab and a "Last updated by X on Y" line on its Role Permissions section;
a "Created by X" / "Updated by Y" line per card on SubscriptionPlans.tsx;
a "Last updated by X on Y" line on PlatformSettings.tsx. Null name (self-
registered user, pre-existing/seeded row predating this field) renders as
"-", matching AuditLog's existing fallback.

Backend-only, no schema change - will verify with dotnet build + the full
test suites on User Service, then move to the frontend changes and
live-verify in the browser before marking DONE.

DONE (2026-09-05): Implemented and verified the plan above. Backend: added

GetByIdsAsync/GetByIdAsync, embedded CreatedByName/UpdatedByName on

and RolePermissionsResponse; added IRolePermissionRepository.
GetForRoleWithMetadataAsync (a new method, not touching the existing
login/refresh-path GetForRoleAsync) so GetTenantRolePermissionsHandler
stopped hardcoding null metadata. Verification: dotnet build clean, User
Service Application.Tests 94/94. Frontend: added the new fields to
types/tenant.ts, types/plan.ts, types/user.ts, types/platformSettings.ts;

pre-existing unrelated warnings), 61/61 frontend tests passed.

Along the way, hit and resolved a real Docker Desktop incident (unrelated
to the code, but blocking the live-verify step): the Docker VM's vhdx had
ballooned to 38GB, almost entirely (22GB) accumulated build cache; the
first rebuild attempt hung and the Docker daemon itself crashed (500 from
the daemon API). Restarted Docker Desktop, ran `docker builder prune -af`
(cache only - zero images/containers/volumes touched, so no data was ever
at risk), brought all 15 containers back up healthy, then rebuilt
user-api + frontend and redeployed. Compacting the actual .vhdx file size
on disk needs admin/UAC elevation this session doesn't have - left as an
optional manual step for the user, not required for functionality.

Live-verified in the browser as the QA Super Admin: created a brand-new
test organization and confirmed both its detail page and the Organizations
list immediately showed "Created By: QA Super Admin" (then deleted the
test org to clean up) - proving the full pipeline end to end (JWT claim ->
